# Arkansas — Election Administrative Rules

Dataset: State Election Rules and Guidance. Official rules content for Arkansas, compiled for the VB Election Desk knowledge base. Every section below cites the official source it was captured from and the publisher's own currency statement; sources were verified as official publications before inclusion.
Publisher: Code of Arkansas Rules — published by the Arkansas Secretary of State under Ark. Code § 25-15-218 — https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/
Sections: 190

## Code of Arkansas Rules, Title 7 — Elections: Chapter II (Secretary of State) and Chapter III (State Board of Election Commissioners)

- **Publisher:** Arkansas Secretary of State (Code of Arkansas Rules)
- **Category:** rules
- **Registered source:** https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=title&titleID=7
- **Listed as current at:** https://www.sos.arkansas.gov/elections/for-rules-regulations
- **Currency (publisher's statement):** Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent."
- **Note:** The Code of Arkansas Rules is the official codification by statute: "This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." The publisher adds: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent."
- **Note:** Captured chapters: Chapter II (Secretary of State — financial disclosure by county board members, voter-registration verification, vote centers, secure voter process, statewide voter registration list security) and Chapter III (State Board of Election Commissioners — administration, complaints, expenses, training, voting). Chapter I (Arkansas Ethics Commission) is not captured: it is campaign finance and ethics, out of scope (Nicholas, Q6).
- **Note:** The publisher's navigation tree is JavaScript-only; sections are enumerated through the publisher's own JSON tree API and each section's page is then read server-side (custom handler supplemental_handlers/arkansas.py). Sections the API marks repealed are skipped.
- **Note:** Each record carries the publisher's History note — the Arkansas Register rule number and effective date (e.g. "Ark. R. 2026-10 (eff. February 1, 2026)") — and its Authority note, appended after the rule text.
- **Note:** Arkansas's other official copy of these rules is the Board's own signed rule PDFs; the Code of Arkansas Rules is the canonical text for this dataset (Nicholas, Q9), so those PDFs are not ingested.

7 CAR § 20-101. Title.
This part shall be known as the “Rules for Financial Disclosure by Members of County Boards of Election Commissioners”.

Authority:

7 CAR § 20-102. Purpose.
The purpose of this part is to provide a form and procedures for the disclosure of financial information by members of county boards of election commissioners.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-119

7 CAR § 20-103. Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “County board of election commissioners” or “CBEC” means the board elected by county committees as prescribed by Arkansas Code § 7-4-102;
(2) “Member” or “members” means a person or persons elected to serve on a county board of election commissioners;
(3)(A) “Form” means the document created by the Secretary of State for financial disclosure by members of county boards of election commissioners.
(B) A copy of the form, titled “Disclosure by Member of a County Board of Election Commissioners” is attached to this part; and
(4) “Spouse” means any person married to a member of a county board of election commissioners during the calendar year for which a financial report is required.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-119

7 CAR § 20-104. General.
Every member of a county board of election commissioners must report by January 31 of each calendar year certain financial information to the Secretary of State as described in Arkansas Code § 7-4-119, as amended by Acts 2013, No. 443.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-119

7 CAR § 20-105. Place of filing.
(a) The mailing address for reporting the information shall be:
Office of the Secretary of State
Elections Division
State Capitol, Room 026
Little Rock, AR 72201
(b) The telephone number to be used for inquiries relating to the procedures for a member of a county board of election commissioners to file financial information is (501) 682-5070.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-119

### 7 CAR § 20-106. Procedure for disclosure

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=500&subPartID=4629&sectionID=29027. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 20-106. Procedure for disclosure.
(a) All members of a county board of election commissioners shall disclose the required financial information using the form prescribed by the Secretary of State.
(b) The CBEC member shall report the following information on the approved form:
(1) The filing year;
(2) CBEC member name;
(3) CBEC member address;
(4) CBEC member city, state, zip;
(5) CBEC member county of residence;
(6) Either check a box stating that during the previous calendar year, the CBEC member has not had any sales referenced in Arkansas Code § 7-4-119, or fill in relevant sales information as required in the blanks provided and include:
(A) Goods or services sold;
(B) Sold to;
(C) Sold by; and
(D) Relationship of CBEC member to seller; and
(7) CBEC member signature.
(c) The required form must be notarized.
(d) The required form must be filed by January 31 of each calendar year.
(e) Forms are to be returned to:
Office of the Arkansas Secretary of State
Elections Division
State Capitol, Room 026
Little Rock, AR 72201

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-119

7 CAR § 21-102. Purpose.
The purpose of this part is to provide procedures for the issuance of a verification of voter registration card and implementation of a program facilitating verification of voter registration by voters.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-103. Definitions

7 CAR § 21-103. Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Application” means an application for a verification of voter registration card;
(2) “County clerk” means the official elected by the voters of a county to serve as county clerk or a member of his or her staff;
(3) “Verification of voter registration” means:
(A) A document or identification card that meets all of the following conditions:
(i) Shows the name of the person to whom the document or identification card was issued;
(ii) Shows a photograph of the person to whom the document or identification card was issued;
(iii) Is issued by the United States, the State of Arkansas, or an accredited postsecondary educational institution in the State of Arkansas; and
(iv) If displaying an expiration date, the document or card is not expired or expired no more than four (4) years before the date of the election in which the person seeks to vote.
(B) Documents or identification cards that comply with the above requirements in subdivision (3)(A) of this section for verification of voter registration may include without limitation the following:
(i) A driver’s license;
(ii) A photo identification card;
(iii) A concealed handgun carry license;
(iv) A United States passport;
(v) An employee badge or identification document issued by an accredited postsecondary educational institution in the State of Arkansas;
(vi) A United States military identification document;
(vii) A public assistance identification card if the card shows a photograph of the person to whom the document or identification card was issued; and
(viii) A verification of voter registration card as provided under Arkansas Code § 7-5-324; and
(4) “Verification of voter registration card” means a card issued by a county clerk in Arkansas in compliance with the requirements of Acts 2017, No. 633, and this part.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

7 CAR § 21-104. Equipment and training.
(a) The Secretary of State will provide to the county clerk the equipment, software, and training needed for producing verification of voter registration cards.
(b) The equipment, software, and training may be provided prior to the effective date of this part to allow a county clerk to begin issuing verification of voter registration cards.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-105. Eligibility

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28152. 7 CAR § 21-105. Eligibility.
(a) A verification of voter registration card shall be issued by the county clerk to an applicant who meets the following requirements:
(1) Does not have another valid form of verification of voter registration; and
(2) Either:
(A) Is registered to vote in the county where he or she is submitting the application; or
(B) Will be at least eighteen (18) years of age at the next election and has submitted a voter registration application that has been received and processed by the county clerk to whom he or she is submitting the application.
(b) The applicant shall sign an affidavit that he or she does not possess a valid form of identification meeting the criteria set forth in Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, § 13.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-106. Application

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28153. 7 CAR § 21-106. Application.
(a) Applicant shall use the form designed and published by the Secretary of State to request a verification of voter registration card.
(b)(1) The office of Secretary of State shall:
(A) Post a copy of the application on its website; and
(B) Make a copy of the application available upon request.
(2) The Secretary of State shall also provide a copy of the application form to each of the seventy-five (75) county clerks in the State of Arkansas.
(c) The application for a verification of voter registration card may request the following information from an applicant:
(1) Full legal name;
(2) Residential street address where the applicant is registered to vote;
(3) Date of birth;
(4) Gender;
(5) Height; and
(6) Eye color.
(d)(1) The application shall include an affidavit that he or she does not possess a card or document that would qualify as verification of voter registration as defined in the Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, § 13.
(2) The affidavit shall specifically include the definition of verification of voter registration.
(e) The application shall include sufficient space for the county clerk to record what type of document or documents were presented by the applicant to demonstrate his or her identity.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-107. Verification by county clerk

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28154. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 21-107. Verification by county clerk.
(a) The county clerk shall require the following before issuing a verification of voter registration card:
(1) Completed application; and
(2) Signature of the applicant on the affidavit swearing or affirming that the applicant does not possess any document or card that could be considered verification of voter registration under Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, § 13.
(b) The county clerk shall require the presentation and verification of the following information before issuing a verification of voter registration card:
(1) A photo or nonphoto identity document which includes both the applicant’s full legal name and date of birth;
(2) Documentation showing the applicant’s name and residential address; and
(3) Evidence that the applicant is registered to vote in the county where he or she is submitting the application, or will be at least eighteen (18) years of age at the next election and has submitted a voter registration application that has been received by the county clerk to whom he or she is submitting the application for a verification of voter registration.
(c)(1) Identity documents, as required in subdivision (b)(1) of this section, must include both the applicant’s full legal name and date of birth.
(2) Documents satisfying the requirement of an identity document may include, but are not limited to, any of the following:
(A) Original birth certificate or certified copy of a birth certificate;
(B) Copy of marriage license application;
(C) Copy of applicant’s state or federal tax return filed for the previous calendar year, with an affidavit of authenticity from the taxpayer;
(D) Paycheck or paycheck stub bearing the imprinted names of the applicant and the applicant’s employer;
(E) An original Medicare or Medicaid statement received by the applicant;
(F) An original annual social security statement received by the applicant for any of the current or preceding four (4) calendar years;
(G) Certified school record or transcript for the current or preceding calendar year;
(H) Naturalization documentation; or
(I) DD-214 form issued by the federal government to members of the military.
(d) Documentation showing the applicant’s name and residential address, as required in subdivision (b)(2) of this section may include, but is not limited to, any of the following:
(1) Utility, cable, internet, or satellite television bill issued within the last sixty (60) days;
(2) Bank statement issued within the last sixty (60) days;
(3) Copy of applicant’s state or federal tax return filed for the previous calendar year with affidavit of authenticity from taxpayer;
(4) Currently valid residential rental contract or receipt for payment made within the last sixty (60) days for residential rent payments, which includes name of landlord;
(5) Homeowners’ insurance policy or bill for the current or preceding calendar year;
(6) Mortgage, payment coupon, deed, or property tax bill for the current or preceding calendar year;
(7) Personal property tax bill for the current or preceding calendar year;
(8) Current automobile registration receipt; or
(9) W-2 issued by applicant’s employer for the preceding calendar year.
(e) The county clerk shall verify that the legal name, date of birth, and residential address on the documents provided as described in subsections (b) – (d) of this section, match the name, date of birth, and address on the voter registration record.
(f) The county clerk shall record on the application submitted by the voter the type of document or documents that were presented by the applicant to demonstrate his or her identity.
(g)(1) If the applicant submits a voter registration application on the same day that he or she requests a verification of voter registration card, the county clerk shall process the voter registration application in their routine manner to allow for validation of the voter registration information.
(2) The verification of voter registration card shall not be prepared until the clerk has processed the voter registration application.
(3) The verification of voter registration card may be provided in person or mailed to the mailing address contained on the voter registration application.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

7 CAR § 21-108. Fee.
(a) No fee or charge shall be requested or collected for the application for, or issuance of, a verification of voter registration card, nor for any required affidavit.
(b) The county clerk shall not require or accept payment for a verification of voter registration card.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-109. Information on card

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28156. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 21-109. Information on card.
(a) A verification of voter registration card shall contain the following information for the voter applying for the card:
(1) Applicant’s full legal name; and
(2) Registrant ID number from the electronic voter registration system maintained by the county clerk.
(b) A verification of voter registration card shall also contain the following:
(1) The name of the county issuing the card;
(2) The date of issuance;
(3) Color photograph of the voter applying for the card; and
(4) A statement establishing that the card is valid only for voting purposes.
(c) If permitted by the software provided by the Secretary of the State, the verification of voter registration card shall contain the following:
(1) A silhouette or outline of the county issuing the card; and
(2) A copy of the signature of the county clerk.
(d) The back of the card shall include information on where to return the card if it is found after being stolen, lost, or misplaced.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-110. Expiration

Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 21-110. Expiration.
(a) The verification of voter registration card shall remain valid as long as the person resides in the same county in which the verification of voter registration card was issued and remains registered to vote. If the voter moves to a different county, the voter must either:
(1) Return the verification of voter registration card to the county clerk in which the card was issued upon the submission of a change in registration form; or
(2) Turn the verification of voter registration card into the county clerk of his or her new residence upon submission of change of registration form.
(b) The clerk shall return the “old” verification of voter registration card to the county clerk’s office that issued the card.
(c) If the voter moves out of the State of Arkansas, the voter shall return the verification of voter registration card to the county clerk from which the card was issued.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-111. Photographs

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28158. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 21-111. Photographs.
(a)(1) Each verification of voter registration card shall include a color photograph of the voter applying for the card taken at the time of issuance.
(2) Absent the application of subsections (b) – (d) of this section, the photograph shall be taken without sunglasses, hat, headgear, or other device that disguises or otherwise conceals the face or head of the applicant.
(b) A waiver may be granted by the county clerk allowing the applicant to wear headgear or a head covering for medical or religious purposes so long as the face is not disguised or otherwise concealed.
(c)(1) Any waiver granted pursuant to subsection (b) of this section must be in writing and signed by the county clerk.
(2) The waiver must be attached to the application and remain in the county clerk’s files with the voter’s application for a verification of voter registration card.
(3) Any such wavier is not transferable, but the clerk may forward a copy of a waiver to the clerk of a new county of registration upon request.
(d) The county clerk shall maintain a copy of the photograph and attach it to the application submitted by the voter.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-112. Duplicates or replacement

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28159. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 21-112. Duplicates or replacement.
(a)(1)(A) If a voter misplaces or loses their verification of voter registration card and requests that a duplicate or replacement be issued, the voter shall submit an affidavit to the county clerk, swearing or affirming that the card previously issued has been misplaced or lost.
(B) The voter shall also include in the affidavit a statement that he or she did not give, transfer, or otherwise transmit the previous verification of voter registration card to another person.
(2) The voter shall complete a new application and provide all of the information required under this part for issuance of a verification of voter registration card.
(b)(1) If a voter changes their address within the county or damages their verification of voter registration card and returns it to the county clerk’s office that issued the card, the clerk may provide a replacement verification of voter registration card as long as the applicant is still a registered voter within the county.
(2) The county clerk shall follow all of the procedures described in this part for destruction of the damaged card.
(c)(1) If a duplicate or replacement card is issued, the county clerk shall make a notation in the voter’s record in the electronic voter registration system that a duplicate or replacement card was issued to the voter.
(2) The date that the duplicate or replacement card was issued shall be included in the notation in the voter’s record in the electronic voter registration system.
(3) The replacement card shall have the new date of issuance.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-113. Change of county

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28160. 7 CAR § 21-113. Change of county.
(a)(1) If a voter changes his or her residential address to a different county and needs a verification of voter registration card, the voter shall submit a new application to the new county clerk’s office where he or she is now registered to vote.
(2) The applicant shall provide all of the information under this part for issuance of a verification of voter registration card.
(b) A voter who changes his or her residential address to a different county may use a verification of voter registration card issued by the clerk in the county where he or she previously resided as a form of identification for obtaining a new verification of voter registration card as long as he or she was:
(1) Still registered at the time of the change of registration; and
(2) Otherwise eligible to vote in the county where he or she previously resided.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-114. Additional county clerk responsibilities

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28161. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 21-114. Additional county clerk responsibilities.
(a) The county clerk must maintain the following:
(1) A copy of every application for a verification of voter registration card;
(2) A copy of the photograph taken of the applicant, with the date taken if different from the date of issuance of the card; and
(3) A copy of any written waiver provided to the applicant.
(b) The photograph shall be scanned and attached to the applicant’s voter registration record in the electronic voter registration system.
(c)(1) The county clerk shall make a notation in the voter’s record in the electronic voter registration system that a verification of voter registration card was issued to the voter.
(2) The date that the card was issued should be included in the notation in the voter’s record in the electronic voter registration system.
(d)(1) By the fifth day of each new calendar quarter, the county clerk shall submit to the Secretary of State a report identifying the number of verification of voter registration cards he or she has issued in the prior calendar quarter.
(2) The method for reporting the number of verification of voter registration cards issued shall be determined by the Secretary of State and may be electronic or paper, at the Secretary of State’s discretion.
(e)(1) The county clerk shall destroy any verification of voter registration card that is returned to the clerk’s office if the card was issued by that county clerk’s office.
(2) If any person returns or submits a verification of voter registration card issued by another county clerk’s office, the county clerk shall transmit or return the card to the county of origin, before sending any report to the office of the Secretary of State as required in subsection (d) of this section.
(f)(1) Prior to destroying a verification of voter registration card, the county clerk shall ensure that a notation has been entered in the electronic voter registration system that the card was returned.
(2) A notation shall also be made on the application that the verification of voter registration card was returned, and from which county the card was returned, if not returned by the voter.
(g)(1) If a duplicate or replacement card is issued, the county clerk shall make a notation in the voter’s record in the electronic voter registration system that a duplicate or replacement card was issued to the voter.
(2) The date that the duplicate or replacement card was issued shall be included in the notation in the voter’s record in the electronic voter registration system.
(h) The county clerk is responsible for inventory, security, and integrity of the equipment and the system used to create the identification cards.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

7 CAR § 21-115. Use of card.
(a) A verification of voter registration card is valid for the sole purpose of providing verification of voter registration for voting purposes in the county in which it was issued.
(b) Each verification of voter registration card issued by a county clerk shall contain a statement establishing that the card is valid only for voting purposes.

### 7 CAR § 21-116. Residents of long-term care facilities exempt

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28163. 7 CAR § 21-116. Residents of long-term care facilities exempt.
(a)(1) A resident of a long-term care or residential care facility licensed by the State of Arkansas is not required to verify his or her voter registration before voting.
(2) In lieu of providing verification of voter registration, a resident is required to provide documentation from the administrator of the facility attesting that the person is a resident of the facility.
(b)(1) A letter, on facility letterhead, dated and signed by the administrator which specifically identifies the full name of the resident in the body of the letter is sufficient to meet the documentation requirement.
(2) The letter is valid for one (1) year after the date it is signed and issued.
(c) The letter issued by the administrator can be used by a resident of a long-term care facility:
(1) During early voting;
(2) On election day at a polling location; and
(3) When voting by absentee ballot.
(d) Residents of long-term care or residential care facilities voting on election day or during early voting may also utilize the documentation of residency for voting in person form provided by the Secretary of State.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

### 7 CAR § 21-117. Verification of voter registration required with absentee ballots

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=501&subPartID=4474&sectionID=28164. 7 CAR § 21-117. Verification of voter registration required with absentee ballots.
(a) When voting by absentee ballot, a voter must submit with the voter statement a copy of a verification of voter registration, as defined by 7 CAR § 21-103(3), unless the voter is:
(1) An active duty member of the uniformed services of the United States or United States Merchant Marine who is absent from the country on election day because of his or her service;
(2) The spouse or dependent of an active duty member of the uniformed services of the United States or United States Merchant Marine who is absent from the country on election day because of the service of the member; or
(3) A resident of a long-term care or residential care facility licensed by the State of Arkansas.
(b) A copy of a verification of registration card issued pursuant to Acts 2017, No. 633, and this part can be submitted by an absentee voter to meet the verification of registration requirement.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324

7 CAR § 22-102. Purpose.
(a) The purpose of this part is to designate equipment and establish standards and procedures for Arkansas counties choosing to implement the optional vote centers.
(b)(1) Nothing in this part shall be interpreted to require counties to use vote centers.
(2) This part shall only be applicable to Arkansas counties that use vote centers on election day.

### 7 CAR § 22-103. Definitions

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=502&subPartID=4475&sectionID=28169. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 22-103. Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “County clerk” means the official elected by the voters of a county to serve as county clerk or a member of his or her staff;
(2) “County board of election commissioners” or “CBEC” means the board of election commissioners elected by county committees as prescribed by Arkansas Code § 7-4-102;
(3)(A) “Electronic poll book” means hardware or software or a combination of hardware and software that allows election officials to view voter registration records and voting information during an election at an early voting location or at a polling site, including a vote center, on election day.
(B) At the county’s discretion, and if technology allows, the county may choose to use iPower Profile software as their electronic poll book;
(4)(A) “Equipment” means any mechanical or electronic equipment, for the purposes of the verification of voter registration records, or the direct recording and tabulation of votes.
(B) Such equipment shall include, but not be limited to:
(i) Electronic poll books;
(ii) Voting machines;
(iii) Bar code printers;
(iv) Ballot marking devices; and
(v) Ballot tabulation devices;
(5) “Precinct” means a geographical area, the boundaries of which are determined by a county board of election commissioners, in order to facilitate voting by the registered voters from that geographical area;
(6) “Secure electronic connection” means an electronic connection between a county’s centralized voter registration records and electronic poll books in a polling location, including vote centers, that prevents:
(A) An elector from voting more than once in a single election; and
(B) Unauthorized access to the computerized voter registration records maintained by the county clerk;
(7) “Vote center” means an election day voting location designated by the county clerk or county board of election commissioners at which a qualified elector from any precinct in the county holding the election may vote; and
(8) “Voting machine or machines” means either:
(A) A direct-recording electronic voting machine that:
(i) Records votes by means of a ballot display provided with mechanical or electro-optical components that might be actuated by the voter;
(ii) Processes the data by means of a computer program;
(iii) Records voting data and ballot images in internal and external memory components; and
(iv) Produces a tabulation of the voting data stored in a removable memory component and on a printed copy; or
(B) A ballot marking device, such as an ExpressVote, that allows a voter to input their choices and records those choices on a paper ballot.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

7 CAR § 22-104. Establishment of vote centers.
(a) The county board of election commissioners or county clerk may only establish one (1) or more vote centers in the county on election day in accordance with Arkansas Code § 7-1-113 and Arkansas Code § 7-5-101 (requiring a local ordinance by the quorum court).
(b)(1) Before establishing a vote center, and at least thirty (30) days before early voting begins, the county clerk or county board of election commissioners shall create and adopt a written plan for operation of one (1) or more vote centers within their county on election day.
(2) The plan shall address each of the issues raised in 7 CAR §§ 22-105 – 22-114 of this part and the following listed items:
(A)(i) Physical location of each polling location, including vote centers, to be used by the county on election day.
(ii) Indicate whether each location meets Americans with Disabilities Act requirements and whether the location adequately allows the voter to personally and secretly execute his or her vote;
(B)(i) Description of the equipment to be used to verify voter registration status at each polling location, including vote centers.
(ii) The description must also include an estimated number of poll books, laptops, or other electronic devices to be used to verify voter registration status at each polling location, including vote centers;
(C)(i) Description of the equipment to be used at each polling location, including vote centers, for recording or tabulating votes cast.
(ii) Include an estimated number of voting machines, printers, counting devices, or other electronic devices used to record or tabulate votes cast at each polling location, including vote centers.
(iii) The description must also include an estimated number of Americans with Disabilities Act compliant voting machines that are to be used at each polling location, including vote centers;
(D) Description of the secure electronic connection available at each polling location, including vote centers; and
(E) Method for having available printed and prenumbered ballots for every precinct ballot style in the county, as well as a display of two (2) samples of each ballot posted on the walls at each polling location, including vote centers.
(c) After the vote center plan is established and adopted, but prior to any elections which utilize vote centers, the following must be filed with the Secretary of State:
(1) A copy of the vote center plan;
(2) A copy of the ordinance passed by the county quorum court; and
(3) The county clerk’s certification that each vote center has access to a secured electronic connection.

Codification Notes:
Americans with Disabilities Act is codified primarily at 42 U.S.C. § 12101 et seq.
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-105. Equipment — Voting machines

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=502&subPartID=4475&sectionID=28171. 7 CAR § 22-105. Equipment — Voting machines.
(a)(1) All voting machines used in a vote center must have the capacity to hold every ballot style available in the county and still report results by precinct.
(2) If a county wishes to use a vote center for a preferential primary, the voting machines must have the capacity to hold and tabulate every ballot style for each primary and nonpartisan election available in the county for that election.
(b)(1) The written plan from the county must include an estimated total number of Ivotronics, ExpressVotes, and all other voting machines available for use in the county.
(2) The plan must also include the county plans for auditing the results of an election, prior to certification, to ensure all media and paper ballots have been properly accounted for and tabulated.
(3) The plan must outline the use of, and accounting for, all electronic media used in the election.
(4) The plan must also describe the care, handling, and tracking of those devices due to equipment limitations.
(c)(1) The plan should indicate:
(A) An estimated number of machines used for early voting; and
(B) Whether those machines will be used on election day.
(2) The plan must also include an estimated number of Ivotronics, ExpressVotes, or other voting machines available for election day polling locations (including vote centers).
(d) The following must also be included in the written plan:
(1) Number of Ivotronics and/or other voting machines available in county;
(2) Number of current polling locations on election day;
(3) Estimated number of Ivotronics, ExpressVotes, or other voting machines currently used in each polling location on election day;
(4) Number of current registered voters assigned to each polling location, including the number of active and inactive voters assigned to each polling location;
(5)(A) County’s projection of number of electors who will vote at each polling site, including voting centers, on election day.
(B) Include projections for preferential primary elections and general elections;
(6)(A) Proposed estimated assignment of voting machines for each polling location in the county, including vote center or centers.
(B) Maximum number of cast ballots stored on a machine must:
(i) Allow for voter turnout; and
(ii) Be able to report ballots by precinct.
(C) It is suggested that there be one (1) direct recording electronic voting machine or Ivotronic machine available for every three hundred (300) registered voters and any variation must be described in the county’s proposed plan.
(D) Outline how the county intends to address potential limitations of electronic media used in the election;
(7) Proposed number of paper ballots for each ballot style for each polling location in the county, including vote center or centers;
(8) Proposed assignment of electronic devices for scanning paper ballots that have been marked by voters that will be used at each polling location, including vote centers (i.e. DS200, M100, or 650);
(9) Proposed plan for tabulating votes cast at all polling locations, including vote centers; and
(10) Estimated number of voting machines not assigned to any specific polling location or vote center on election day, but which would be available for use in case of an emergency or high voter turnout at one (1) or more polling places vote centers.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-106. Equipment — Verifying registration records

7 CAR § 22-106. Equipment — Verifying registration records.
(a)(1) If a county chooses to operate one (1) or more vote centers on election day, an electronic poll book or other electronic device must be used in all polling locations on election day to:
(A) Verify the registration of potential voters; and
(B) Prevent an elector from voting more than once.
(2) The electronic poll book or other electronic device must be capable of instantaneously updating voter information at a central location and, between all polling locations on election day, to prevent an individual elector from voting more than once.
(b) The written plan from the county must identify, describe, and provide the specifications for the equipment that the county will use for these purposes.
(c) The written plan from the county must include the estimated number of electronic poll books or other electronic devices for verifying registration records that will be used in each polling location, including vote centers.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-107. Maintenance and use of equipment

7 CAR § 22-107. Maintenance and use of equipment.
(a) The following standards shall apply for maintenance of the equipment in a vote center:
(1) Counties shall ensure that the equipment is:
(A) Properly maintained with current and up-to-date firmware and software; and
(B) Used within the manufacturer’s suggested tolerances;
(2) Equipment used in vote centers is subject to the same standard maintenance procedures for all voting systems in the State of Arkansas; and
(3)(A) If required for maintenance, error with equipment, or break down of any equipment, a vendor or certified technician may perform maintenance on any equipment as needed.
(B) Any maintenance needed on election day must be authorized by the county board of election commissioners.
(b) The following standards shall apply for use of the equipment in a vote center:
(1) Counties shall allow only trained personnel to operate or maintain any of the equipment in a vote center;
(2) All electronic or mechanical equipment is for official use by the county clerks if designated to conduct early voting, or the county board of election commissioners or other designated county personnel; and
(3)(A) Equipment shall be used only for performance of official election duties.
(B) No personal use of equipment is authorized.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-108. Testing and backup of equipment

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=502&subPartID=4475&sectionID=28174. 7 CAR § 22-108. Testing and backup of equipment.
(a) Equipment used at the vote center shall be properly tested and backed up.
(b)(1) Voting machines used at a vote center shall be tested in accordance with Arkansas law for testing of voting machines used at other polling locations.
(2) All statutory deadlines for public testing of the machines, and logic and accuracy testing of the machines, shall be followed for equipment used at vote centers.
(3) All direct recording electronic voting machines shall be equipped with a voter-verified paper audit trail.
(4) The county shall provide sufficient supplies for operation of the voting systems including adequate paper for use of the voter-verified paper audit trail.
(c)(1) All equipment used to verify the registration of potential voters shall be tested at least seven (7) days prior to the start of early voting.
(2) The county shall put sample data on each of the devices during testing to ensure that the device is capable of holding all of the data.
(3)(A) The county shall also test the electronic connection at each polling location, including vote centers, at least seven (7) days prior to the start of early voting.
(B) During the testing of the electronic connection, the county shall update voter information at least three (3) times to ensure that data is capable of being received at the central location and subsequently verified at each polling location, including vote centers.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-109. Secure electronic connections

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=502&subPartID=4475&sectionID=28175. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 22-109. Secure electronic connections.
(a)(1) Acts 2013, No. 1389, requires that counties desiring to utilize one (1) or more vote centers have a sufficient secure electronic connection which prevents:
(A) An elector from voting more than once; and
(B) Unauthorized access to a computerized registration book maintained by the county clerk.
(2)(A) If a vote center is used in an election, the vote center shall have a secure electronic connection to provide and receive voting information to and from the computerized voter registration records maintained by the county clerk.
(B) The secure electronic connection must allow live streaming of information to and from the vote center, and to and from every other polling location, so that double voting is not allowed.
(b)(1) If the county is using one (1) or more vote centers, every polling location, including the vote centers, must have high speed internet available within three hundred feet (300’) of the room where voting will occur in order to allow live updates of electronic poll books and/or any other equipment used to verify the registration of potential voters, to prevent an elector from voting more than once in that election.
(2)(A) The vote center plan must include a description of the electronic connections used in every polling location, including vote centers.
(B) Such description shall also include what type of network, if any, is used.
(c)(1) All information transmitted to or from the county voter registration system to an electronic poll book or other electronic system designated by the county to verify voter registration shall be secure.
(2)(A) The vote center plan must identify the measures to be taken to ensure that there is sufficient security.
(B) At a minimum:
(i) The county shall have a plan for restricting access to the electronic poll books including a login/password plan; and
(ii) All data transfers shall meet, at a minimum, at least one (1) single-layer security protocol and requirements as may be designated by the Secretary of State from time to time.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-110. Electronic/internet system failure

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=502&subPartID=4475&sectionID=28176. 7 CAR § 22-110. Electronic/internet system failure.
(a) Since polling locations, including vote centers, rely heavily on electronic connectivity, each county must have a specific plan for voting should there be a failure of the primary electronic system or internet connectivity.
(b)(1) At a minimum, the county plan must meet the following standards:
(A)(i) Back-up for at least twelve (12) hours of operation for all equipment.
(ii) Batteries, a generator, or other appropriate devices may be used for back-up;
(B) Telephone, either land-line or cell phone, for poll workers using an electronic poll book in order to contact the county clerk’s office to verify voter registration in the event of electronic system failure or other internet connectivity issues;
(C) List of land-line or cell phone numbers for each polling site in the county, including vote centers, in order to contact other polling sites to verify an elector has not previously voted; and
(D)(i) Paper copy of the precinct voter registration list.
(ii) A vote center must have a paper copy of the precinct voter registration list for all precincts within the county.
(iii) As an alternative, a vote center can have a blank precinct voter registration list for voters to complete and sign if the vote center also has the following:
(a)
A complete list of all names of registered voters in the county in alphabetical order and with precinct designated; and
(b)
Individual “sign-in” sheets for voters to complete requiring the voter’s address, precinct, date of birth, and signature.
(2) The county plan:
(A) Shall address each of the items mentioned above; and
(B) Should include any additional steps the county will take if there is an electronic system or internet connectivity failure.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

7 CAR § 22-111. Notification to voters.
(a)(1) Vote center plans shall include the county’s plan for educating voters on the availability of vote center or centers.
(2) If, as part of its plan, the county eliminates certain polling locations, the plan shall include information on how voters will be notified of the polling locations that are closed.
(3) The plan shall include, but not be limited to:
(A) Direct mailings to voters;
(B) Media; and
(C) Signage at current and former polling locations.
(b) All changes shall be subject to publication and notification requirements found in Arkansas Code § 7-5-101.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

7 CAR § 22-112. Ordinance required
.
(a) When the plan has been adopted and the county clerk has certified to the Secretary of State and the county quorum court that the county has sufficient secure electronic connections at each polling location to meet the requirements of Arkansas Code § 7-5-101(e)(1)(A), the county may adopt an ordinance to establish one (1) or more vote centers.
(b) A copy of the ordinance shall be filed with the:
(1) County clerk;
(2) County board of election commissioners; and
(3) Secretary of State.
(c) An ordinance must be approved by the county quorum court and a copy filed with the county clerk, the county board of election commissioners, and the Secretary of State before a county may operate a vote center.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-113. Provisional ballots

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=502&subPartID=4475&sectionID=28179. 7 CAR § 22-113. Provisional ballots.
(a) If a potential voter seeking to cast his or her ballot at a vote center is not listed in the county voter registration files, the registration cannot be verified by the county clerk or election official, and the voter contends that he or she is eligible to vote, the voter shall be given a provisional ballot.
(b) A vote center must have provisional ballots available for all precincts in the county and copies of all ballots must be posted on the wall as sample ballots as required by law.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

### 7 CAR § 22-114. Change of vote center location

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=502&subPartID=4475&sectionID=28180. 7 CAR § 22-114. Change of vote center location.
(a) Except for school elections under Arkansas Code § 6-14-106, a vote center location for each election shall be the same as that established for the immediately preceding general election unless changed by order of the county board of election commissioners.
(b) The county board of election commissioners shall not change a vote center location fewer than thirty (30) days before an election except in the event of an emergency.
(c) Notice of a change made to a vote center location shall be posted at the voter center location used in the last election, and except for school elections and special elections, the notice shall be published in a newspaper of general circulation in the county at least fifteen (15) days before the election.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-1-113

7 CAR § 23-101. Purpose.
The purpose of this part is to create the process by which registered voters who are victims of domestic violence may shield their address or addresses from public view by becoming secure voters.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-112

### 7 CAR § 23-102. Definitions

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=503&subPartID=4630&sectionID=29029. 7 CAR § 23-102. Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Poll watcher” means any:
(A) Candidate in person, but only during the counting and tabulation of ballots and the processing of absentee ballots;
(B) Authorized representative of a candidate;
(C) Authorized representative of a group seeking the passage or defeat of a measure on the ballot; and
(D) Authorized representative of a political party with a candidate on the ballot; and
(2) “Secure voter” means a registered voter who requests to have his or her address or addresses protected from public release and is a victim of domestic violence according to the documentation required by 7 CAR § 23-103(d).

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-112

### 7 CAR § 23-103. Secure voter registration

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=111&subChapterID=143&partID=503&subPartID=4630&sectionID=29030. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 23-103. Secure voter registration.
(a) Unless otherwise stated in this part, a registered voter designated as a secure voter is required to comply with all voter identification requirements when casting a ballot.
(b)(1) A registered voter may apply to be a secure voter no later than thirty (30) days before election day at the office of the county clerk of the county in which they are registered to vote.
(2) A registered voter may request to be a secure voter after the thirty-day cutoff but no later than fifteen (15) days before election day only if an approved court order has been issued after the thirty-day cutoff period.
(c) The county clerk shall verify a voter’s eligibility for secure voter status by comparing the applicant’s name and address as listed on the applicant’s voter registration to the applicant’s name and address as listed on the applicant’s documentation required for approval for secure voter status, as listed in subsection (d) of this section.
(d) The county clerk shall not grant secure voter status to a registered voter unless the registered voter has provided documentation proving one (1) of the following:
(1) He or she is the victim of any offense under Arkansas Code § 5-26-301 et seq. as adjudicated by a court;
(2) He or she has been granted an order of protection under the Domestic Abuse Act of 1991, Arkansas Code § 9-15-101 et seq.; or
(3) He or she is recognized as a victim of domestic violence in any court order or ruling.
(e) Any and all address information of the secure voter shall not be included in the precinct voter registration list.
(f) The county clerk shall not reproduce any address or addresses of a secure voter in any format unless otherwise authorized by law.
(g) Any and all address information for a secure voter is confidential and is not a public record under the Freedom of Information Act of 1967, Arkansas Code § 25-19-101 et seq.
(h) Any documentation required by this part provided to the county clerk shall be scanned electronically and saved with the secure voter’s registration.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-112

7 CAR § 23-104. Challenges.
(a) Upon a challenge from an authorized poll watcher, the secure voter shall be permitted to present proof to the poll worker and poll watcher in a separate room or, if a separate room is not available, a private area located at the polling site or vote center.
(b) If a provisional ballot is required, any and all address information of the secure voter shall be redacted prior to any public release of the ballot itself.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-5-112

### 7 CAR § 24-101. Secretary of State duties

Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 24-101. Secretary of State duties.
The Secretary of State shall:
(1) Cooperate with other states and jurisdictions to compare registered voters, voter history, and voter registration lists to:
(A) Ensure the accuracy of the voter registration rolls;
(B) Identify voters whose addresses have changed;
(C) Prevent registration in more than one (1) state; and
(D) Determine the eligibility of individuals to vote in Arkansas;
(2) Have the authority to utilize any services available to establish and implement a system for the verification of citizenship status for a person registering to vote;
(3)(A) Regularly and before each election identify registered voters who are deceased by comparing the information received from the Social Security Administration, including without limitation a master death file or index compiled by the Social Security Administration.
(B) The Secretary of State shall obtain monthly updates of the Limited Access Death Master File (LADMF) from the National Technical Information Service (NTIS), United States Department of Commerce, in an electronic format.
(C) Upon receipt of the electronic data from NTIS, the Secretary of State shall utilize an electronic delivery service to send those records to each county clerk.
(D) To assist in each county’s compliance with the National Voter Registration Act, 52 U.S.C. § 20501 et seq., the Secretary of State shall send records to the county clerks that only pertain to deceased voters in their respective county.
(E) The Secretary of State shall send the electronic data in batch form;
(4) Verify voter registration address information by the following process:
(A) In addition to the process outlined in subdivision (1) of this section, the Secretary of State shall obtain a monthly report from the United States Postal Service National Change of Address (NCOA) program;
(B) The Secretary of State shall compare the voter registration address currently on file and the addresses obtained from NCOA and other states to determine any changes; and
(C) After reviewing the reports received, the Secretary of State shall send voter registration application forms to voters who have recently changed addresses or updated their address with NCOA; and
(5) Provide annual reports to the Joint Performance Review Committee concerning the accuracy of the voter registration record files.

Authority:
Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 7

(a) All confidential voter registration information and data is confidential.
(b) County boards of election commissioners shall have view-only access to the voter registration record files and data.

7 CAR § 50-101.
Scope.
(a) This part sets forth a general description of the State Board of Election Commissioners, stating the general course and method of the board’s operations and the nature and requirements of the board’s formal and informal procedures pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-203.
(b) This part also sets forth the procedure for the filing and disposition of petitions for declaratory orders as to the applicability of any rule, statute, or order enforced by the board pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-206.
(c) This part is in addition to, and not a substitute for, the laws of the State of Arkansas.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

### 7 CAR § 50-102. Board organization

Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 50-102.
Board organization.
(a)(1) The State Board of Election Commissioners is an agency of the state government composed of seven appointed commissioners and a staff.
(2) The entities who appoint board members, the qualifications of board members, the terms of board members, and other duties and responsibilities of the board are set out in Arkansas Code § 7-4-101.
(b)(1) The legislature has:
(A) Empowered the board to enforce:
(i) Election laws; and
(ii) Voter registration laws; and
(B) Delegated to the board the authority to promulgate rules to ensure:
(i) Even and consistent application of voter registration laws; and
(ii) Fair and orderly election procedures.
(2) The board also:
(A) Develops resources to educate and assist:
(i) Candidates; and
(ii) County election administrators;
(B) Develops specialized training programs;
(C) Conducts and coordinates statewide training of county election commissioners and election officials;
(D) Monitors compliance by local election authorities with federal and state election laws;
(E) Investigates complaints of alleged:
(i) Election misconduct; and
(ii) Election law violations; and
(F) Distributes funds to the counties for state-supported:
(i) Political party primary elections;
(ii) Nonpartisan general elections;
(iii) Special primary elections; and
(iv) Statewide special elections.
(c)(1) The Secretary of State is the Chair of the State Board of Election Commissioners and the Secretary of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(2) The chair or a person designated by the chair conducts meetings of the board.
(3) The chair’s designee:
(A) Is the chair’s proxy;
(B) Counts toward a quorum to conduct business; and
(C) May vote in the meeting.
(d)(1) The board may form such committees or subcommittees as it deems necessary or appropriate to accomplish its legal aims and statutory purposes.
(2) The board may:
(A) Appoint one (1) or more of its members to an unofficial committee or subcommittee; and
(B) Invite nonboard members to participate as unofficial committee or subcommittee members.
(e) At any meeting of the board, four (4) members constitute a quorum, whether:
(1) In person; or
(2) By electronic or telephonic means.
(f)(1) A majority of the members of the board present shall vote on any matter before the board for any decision of the board to become effective.
(2) However, no sanctions for violations of election laws or voter registration laws may be imposed without the affirmative vote of at least four (4) members.
(g) The following matters shall be by motion, followed by a second, for a vote of the board to be valid:
(1) All matters requiring affirmative action by:
(A) The full board; or
(B) One (1) of its members;
(2) All decisions on public positions taken by the full board;
(3) All decisions on declaratory orders; and
(4) All decisions regarding findings of fact and conclusions of law or other actions regarding a person accused of violating the board’s rules or laws under the board’s jurisdiction.
(h) The board may by majority vote delegate the execution of any official duty or action to:
(1) Its chair;
(2) One (1) of the other members; or
(3) The Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(i) A vacancy on the board shall not impair the right of the board to exercise its statutory powers and authority, subject to the requirement that a quorum be present and participating before any action of the board shall be considered valid.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained the following at the end of subsection (c): “[A.C.A. § 7-4-101(e)(4)]”.
This section contained the following at the end of subsection (f): "[A.C.A. § 7-4-101(e)(3)]".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

7 CAR § 50-103.
Staff organization.
(a)(1) The State Board of Election Commissioners may employ a director who may hire a staff.
(2) The Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners or other members of the staff may:
(A) Act as spokespeople for the board;
(B) Receive correspondence and correspond on behalf of the board;
(C) Provide legal and other research for the board;
(D) Investigate allegations of violations of election laws under the board’s jurisdiction; and
(E) Perform other functions deemed appropriate by the board.
(3) The director serves at the will and the pleasure of the board.
(b) The director shall ensure that the Chair of the State Board of Election Commissioners is advised of the:
(1) Progress and conduct of the employees; and
(2) Operation of the board office.
(c)(1) The director shall operate the board’s office.
(2) The director’s duties shall include, without limitation, the following:
(A) General administration of the office and staff;
(B) Managing the appropriated budget of the board;
(C) Maintenance of the board’s files and records as the custodian of those records;
(D) Preparing:
(i) Reports;
(ii) Studies;
(iii) Policy statements;
(iv) Legal interpretations;
(v) Proposed rules;
(vi) Training materials;
(vii) Reimbursements of county election expenses; and
(viii) Other matters required by the board;
(E) Receiving and responding to correspondence on behalf of the board on matters relating to official board business;
(F) Managing travel, logistics, expenses, and reimbursement for members and staff and scheduling seminars and training by or for the board; and
(G) Ensuring that the board office and its employees:
(i) Reflect good character;
(ii) Comply with the law; and
(iii) Carry out other duties as may, from time to time, be enumerated by the board.
(d) Employees of the office (the staff) are the responsibility of the director in matters common to an employer-employee relationship, and the director shall be responsible to the board for the actions of staff.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

### 7 CAR § 50-104. Meeting times and sites

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=305&partID=1170&subPartID=6578&sectionID=42805. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 50-104.
Meeting times and sites.
(a)(1) Meetings shall be called:
(A) As needed by the Chair of the State Board of Election Commissioners; or
(B) Upon written request to the chair of at least four (4) members of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(2) The Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners shall notify all members of the date, time, and place of the meeting.
(b)(1) A written request by four (4) members for a meeting:
(A) Shall state the date of the meeting;
(B) Shall state the items to be taken up at the meeting;
(C) Shall be delivered to:
(i) The chair;
(ii) The director; and
(iii) All other members; and
(D) May be delivered as a:
(i) Separate email from each member requesting the meeting; or
(ii) Letter or letters signed by one (1) or more of the members requesting the meeting.
(2) Letters may be delivered by:
(A) Hand;
(B) First-class mail through the United States Post Office; or
(C) Commercial mail service.
(3) Upon receipt of the written request, the director shall:
(A) Notify all members of the date, time, and place of the meeting; and
(B) Prepare an agenda in consultation with the chair for the meeting that includes the items in the written request.
(c) Notice of the date, time, and place of all meetings shall be furnished to:
(1) Anyone who requests the information;
(2) The news media located in the county in which the meeting is to be held; and
(3) Those located elsewhere that:
(A) Cover meetings of the board; and
(B) Have requested to be so notified.
(d)(1) Notice of meetings shall be provided to the news media and others who request notice:
(A) At least forty-eight (48) hours prior to the scheduled meeting; or
(B) As soon as possible after the meeting is called if it is called within forty-eight (48) hours of the scheduled meeting.
(2) No meeting shall be held until at least two (2) hours after the news media has been notified of the date, time, and place of the meeting.
(e) The board may vary its meeting site to:
(1) Accommodate its needs; and
(2) Accomplish its purposes.
(f)(1) The director shall prepare a proposed agenda for each meeting with the consent of the chair.
(2) The proposed agenda shall be distributed to the board members prior to any such meeting.
(3) Agenda items may be added during meetings by a majority vote of the board.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained the following at the end of subsection (a): “[A.C.A. § 7-4-101(e)]”.
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

7 CAR § 50-105.
Meeting procedures.
(a) All meetings of the State Board of Election Commissioners shall be governed by Robert’s Rules of Order, except as may be modified by a majority of the board or by rule.
(b)(1) Any member who has an actual conflict of interest in any proceeding before the board shall recuse voluntarily from any involvement in the matter.
(2) If four (4) members determine that a member has a conflict, they may, by affirmative vote, disqualify said member from participation in the matter.
(c)(1) All meetings of the board shall be open to the public.
(2) All records generated or accepted by the board and its staff shall be open to public inspection, except for:
(A) Matters that are parts of a complaint record while the record is exempt from disclosure under Arkansas Code § 7-4-120(c)(2); and
(B) The advice and opinions of the board’s attorneys on matters that may be considered to have occurred in the attorney-client relationship.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

7 CAR § 50-106.
Declaratory orders defined.
(a) A declaratory order is a means of resolving a controversy or answering questions or doubts concerning the applicability of statutory provisions, rules, or orders over which the State Board of Election Commissioners has authority.
(b) A petition for declaratory order may be used only to resolve questions or doubts as to how the statutes, rules, or orders may apply to the petitioner’s particular circumstances.
(c) A petition must describe the potential impact of statutes, rules, or orders upon the petitioner’s interests.

7 CAR § 50-107.
Petition for a declaratory order.
(a) The process to obtain a declaratory order is begun by filing a petition that provides:
(1) The name, address, telephone number, and signature of the person requesting the order; and
(2) A complete statement of the facts and circumstances applicable to that person, including the statutes, rules, or orders for which clarification is sought.
(b) The petitioner may request a hearing in the petition.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

### 7 CAR § 50-108. Declaratory order

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=305&partID=1170&subPartID=6578&sectionID=42809. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 50-108.
Declaratory order.
(a)(1) Staff shall promptly draft an order.
(2) A draft order prepared by staff is unofficial until it has received the affirmative vote of a majority of a quorum of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(3) The board may rely on the statements of fact set out in the petition without taking any position with regard to the validity of the facts.
(b)(1) No declaratory order shall be valid, official, or have any effect unless it has been approved by a majority vote of a quorum of the board.
(2) The board may:
(A) Alter, amend, or strike any portion of the draft order; or
(B) Table a draft order for consideration in a future meeting or request that it be rewritten to comply with the commission’s directives.
(3) The board will render a final order within ninety (90) days of filing the petition, either:
(A) Denying the petition; or
(B) Issuing a declaratory order.
(4) The board may reconsider, withdraw, or amend prior orders on its own motion or by petition of a citizen, on a majority vote of a quorum of the board.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

### 7 CAR § 50-109. Filing an appeal of a polling site closure

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=305&partID=1170&subPartID=6578&sectionID=42810. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10).
