7 CAR § 61-102. Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Complainant" means any person who:
(A) Believes that a violation of any provision of Title III:
(i) Has occurred;
(ii) Is occurring; or
(iii) Is about to occur; and
(B) Files a complaint with the State Board of Election Commissioners;
(2) "Respondent" means any state or local election official whose actions are asserted, in a complaint filed with the State Board of Election Commissioners, to be in violation of Title III; and
(3) "Title III" means Title III of the Help America Vote Act of 2002, Pub. L. No. 107-252, 116 Stat. 1666 (2002), codified at 52 U.S.C. §§ 21081-21102.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

7 CAR § 61-103. Who may file.
Any person who believes that a violation of any provision of Title III has occurred, is occurring, or is about to occur may file a complaint.

Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

7 CAR § 61-104. Form of complaint.
A complaint shall be in writing, notarized, signed, and sworn by the complainant.

7 CAR § 61-105. Filing a complaint.
(a)
Place for filing.
A complaint shall be filed with the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b)
Time for filing.
A complaint shall be filed within thirty (30) days after the occurrence of the actions or events that form the basis for the complaint, including the actions or events that form the basis for the complainant’s belief that a violation is about to occur, or, if later, within thirty (30) days after the complainant knew or, with the exercise of reasonable diligence, should have known of those actions or events.
(c)
Copy for respondent.
The complainant shall mail or deliver a copy of the complaint to each respondent.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

### 7 CAR § 61-106. Processing a complaint

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=145&partID=505&subPartID=4571&sectionID=28764. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 61-106. Processing a complaint.
(a)
Consolidation.
The State Board of Election Commissioners may consolidate complaints if they relate to the same actions or events or if they raise common questions of law or fact.
(b)
Record.
(1) The State Board of Election Commissioners shall compile and maintain an official record in connection with each complaint filed in accordance with 7 CAR §§ 61-104 and 61-105 of this part.
(2) The official record shall contain:
(A) A copy of the complaint, including any amendments made with the permission of the State Board of Election Commissioners;
(B) A copy of any written submission by the complainant;
(C) A copy of any written response by any respondent or other interested person;
(D) A written report of any investigation conducted by members or employees of the State Board of Election Commissioners or of any local board of election commissioners, who may not be directly involved in the actions or events complained of and may not directly supervise or be directly supervised by any respondent;
(E) Copies of all notices and correspondence to or from the State Board of Election Commissioners in connection with the complaint;
(F) Originals or copies of any tangible evidence produced at any hearing conducted under subsection (c) of this section;
(G) The original tape recording produced at any hearing conducted under subsection (c) of this section, and a copy of any transcript obtained by:
(i) The State Board of Election Commissioners;
(ii) Any local board of election commissioners; or
(iii) Any other party; and
(H) A copy of any final determination made by the State Board of Election Commissioners under subsection (d) or (e) of this section.
(c)
Hearing.
(1) At the request of the complainant, there shall be a hearing on the record.
(2) The complainant must submit within thirty (30) days of filing the complaint a written request for a hearing.
(3) The hearing shall be conducted no later than sixty (60) days after the State Board of Election Commissioners receives the complaint.
(4) The Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners shall give at least five (5) business days' advance notice of the date, time, and place of the hearing:
(A) By mail to:
(i) The complainant;
(ii) Each named respondent; and
(iii) Any other interested person who has asked in writing to be advised of the hearing;
(B) By posting in a prominent place, available to the general public, at the office of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(5) The director or the director’s designee shall act as hearing officer.
(6) The complainant, any respondent, or any other interested member of the public may:
(A) Appear at the hearing and testify; or
(B) Present tangible evidence in connection with the complaint.
(7) Each witness shall be sworn.
(8) The hearing officer may limit the testimony, if necessary, to ensure that all interested participants are able to present their views.
(9) The hearing officer may recess the hearing and reconvene at a later date, time, and place announced publicly at the hearing.
(10) A complainant, respondent, or other person who testifies or presents evidence at the hearing may, but need not, be represented by an attorney.
(11)(A) There shall be no right of cross-examination.
(B) A person may testify or present evidence to contradict any other testimony or evidence.
(C) If a person has already testified or presented evidence at the hearing and wishes to contradict testimony or evidence subsequently presented, that person is not entitled to be heard again, but may make a written presentation to the hearing officer.
(12)(A) The proceedings shall be tape-recorded by the director.
(B) The recording shall not be transcribed as a matter of course, but the State Board of Election Commissioners, a local board of election commissioners, or any party may obtain a transcript at its own expense.
(C) If a local board of election commissioners or other party obtains a transcript, the board or party shall file a copy as part of the record, and any other interested person may examine the record copy.
(13)(A) Any party to the proceedings may file a written brief or memorandum within five (5) business days after the conclusion of the hearing.
(B) No responsive or reply memoranda will be accepted, except with the specific authorization of the hearing officer.
(d)
Final determination.
(1) If the complaint is not filed timely or in proper form, the State Board of Election Commissioners, acting through the director or the director’s designee, shall dismiss the complaint.
(2)(A) If there has been no hearing under subsection (c) of this section, the director or the director’s designee shall review the record and determine whether, under a preponderance of the evidence standard, a violation of Title III has been established.
(B) The determination of the director or the director’s designee shall be the determination of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(3)(A) At the conclusion of any hearing under subsection (c) of this section, the hearing officer shall determine, under a preponderance of the evidence standard, whether a violation of Title III has been established.
(B) The determination of the hearing officer shall be the determination of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(4)(A) If the director or the director’s designee, whether acting as hearing officer or otherwise, determines that a violation has occurred, the State Board of Election Commissioners, acting through the director or director’s designee, shall provide the appropriate remedy.
(B) The remedy may include, but is not limited to, a determination directing the respondent to take specified action with respect to a past or future election.
(C) The remedy may not include an award of money damages or attorney's fees.
(5) If the director or the director’s designee, whether acting as hearing officer or otherwise, determines that a violation has not occurred or that there is insufficient evidence to establish a violation, the State Board of Election Commissioners, acting through the director or director’s designee, shall dismiss the complaint.
(6) The director or the director’s designee shall explain in a written decision the reasons for the determination and for any remedy selected.
(7)(A) Either the complainant or the respondent may appeal the decision of the director or the director’s designee by submitting a written request to the director within three (3) days of receipt of the decision.
(B) The members of the State Board of Election Commissioners may review the record compiled in connection with the complaint, including the tape recording or any transcript of a hearing and any briefs or memoranda, but shall not receive additional testimony or evidence.
(C) In exceptional cases, the State Board of Election Commissioners may request that the parties present additional briefs or memoranda.
(D) The State Board of Election Commissioners shall issue a written decision affirming, reversing, or modifying the decision being appealed within five (5) days of the State Board of Election Commissioners’ review.
(8)(A) Except as specified in subdivision (d)(9) of this section, the final determination shall be issued within ninety (90) days after the complaint was filed, unless the complainant consents in writing to an extension.
(B) The final determination shall be mailed to:
(i) The complainant;
(ii) Each respondent; and
(iii) Any other interested person who has asked in writing to be advised of the final determination.
(C) The final determination shall be:
(i) Published on the State Board of Election Commissioners’ website; and
(ii) Made available on request to any interested person.
(9)(A) If a final determination is not made within ninety (90) days after the complaint was filed, or within any extension to which the complainant consents, the complaint shall be referred for final resolution under subsection (e) of this section.
(B) The record compiled under subsection (b) of this section shall be made available for use under subsection (e) of this section.
(e)
Alternate dispute resolution.
(1) If, for any reason, the director or the director’s designee does not render a final determination within ninety (90) days after the complaint was filed, or within any extension to which the complainant consents, the complaint shall be resolved under this subsection.
(2)(A) On or before the fifth business day after a final State Board of Election Commissioners’ determination was due, the State Board of Election Commissioners shall designate in writing to the complainant the name of an arbitrator to serve on a panel to resolve the complaint.
(B) Within three (3) business days after the complainant receives this designation, the complainant shall designate in writing to the State Board of Election Commissioners the name of a second arbitrator.
(C) Within three (3) business days after the complainant’s designation, the two (2) arbitrators so designated shall select a third arbitrator to complete the panel.
(3) The arbitrator shall be selected from the certified list published by the Arkansas Alternative Dispute Resolution Commission.
(4)(A) The arbitration panel may review the record compiled in connection with the complaint, including the tape recording or any transcript of a hearing and any briefs or memoranda, but shall not receive additional testimony or evidence.
(B) In exceptional cases, the panel may request that the parties present additional briefs or memoranda.
(5) The arbitrators shall determine the appropriate resolution of the complaint by a majority vote.
(6)(A) The arbitration panel must issue a written resolution within sixty (60) days after the final State Board of Election Commissioners determination was due under subsection (d) of this section.
(B) This sixty (60) day period may not be extended.
(C) The final resolution of the panel shall:
(i) Be transmitted to the State Board of Election Commissioners; and
(ii) Be the final resolution of the complaint.
(D) The final resolution shall be mailed to:
(i) The complainant;
(ii) Each respondent; and
(iii) Any other interested person who has asked in writing to be advised of the final resolution.
(E) The final resolution shall be:
(i) Published on the State Board of Election Commissioners’ website; and
(ii) Made available on request to any interested person.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

7 CAR § 70-101. Scope.
(a) This part sets forth general procedures for distribution of funds from the state to the counties for election expenses related to state-funded elections.
(b) This part is in addition to and not a substitute for the laws of the State of Arkansas.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 70-102. Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Canvassing” means examining and counting the returns of votes cast at a public election to determine authenticity;
(2) “County board” means the county board of election commissioners;
(3) “Election expenses” means costs incurred by a county for conducting a state-funded election, poll workers’ pay, extra help pay, mileage expenses, election commissioners’ pay, supply costs, programming costs, ballot printing costs, legal advertising costs, and facility fees, as specified in this part established by the State Board of Election Commissioners;
(4) “Election official” means:
(A) A member of the county board of election commissioners;
(B) One who performs election coordinator duties;
(C) One who is a poll worker designated by a county board of election commissioners to be an:
(i) Election clerk;
(ii) Election judge; or
(iii) Election sheriff;
(D) A deputy county clerk; or
(E) A person assigned by a county clerk to conduct early voting;
(5) “Electronic vote tabulating device” means a device, commonly referred to as an optical scanner, used to electronically scan a marked paper ballot for the purpose of tabulation;
(6)(A) “Extra help” means temporary full-time, temporary part-time, or contract labor employed by a county to assist the county board of election commissioners.
(B) For the purpose of this part, a “temporary county employee” is defined as any person employed only during limited parts of the year to assist county election commissions in conducting elections;
(7) “Nonpartisan general election” means the regular biennial election held on the same date and at the same time and places as for a preferential primary election for election of a:
(A) Justice of the Supreme Court;
(B) Judge of the Court of Appeals;
(C) Circuit judge;
(D) Prosecuting attorney; and
(E) District judge;
(8) “Offsite early voting” means polling sites for early voting that are:
(A) Located outside the office of the county clerk; and
(B) Under the direction and supervision of the county board of election commissioners;
(9) “Permanent county employee” means any person who is not “extra help” as defined by this part, one who is employed by the county on a permanent full-time or permanent part-time basis;
(10) “Poll worker” means an election official designated by the county board of election commissioners to be an election clerk, election judge, or election sheriff at a polling site;
(11) “Polling site” means a location selected by the county board of election commissioners where votes are cast;
(12) “Primary election” means any election held to select the nominees of a political party for election at any general or special election in this state;
(13) “Public meeting” means any gathering consistent with the definition established under the Freedom of Information Act of 1967, Arkansas Code § 25-19-101 et seq.;
(14) “Special election” means any specially scheduled election to:
(A) Fill vacancies; or
(B) Approve any measure;
(15) “State board” means the State Board of Election Commissioners;
(16) “State-funded elections” means:
(A) Preferential primary elections;
(B) General primary elections;
(C) Special primary elections;
(D) Nonpartisan general elections; and
(E) Statewide special elections;
(17) “Vote center” means an election day location designated by the county board of election commissioners at which a qualified elector from any precinct in the county holding the election may vote; and
(18)(A) “Voting system” means the total combination of mechanical, electromechanical, or electronic equipment, including the software, firmware, and documentation required to program, control, and support the equipment that is used to:
(i) Define ballots;
(ii) Cast and count votes;
(iii) Report or display election results; and
(iv) Maintain and produce any audit trail information.
(B) “Voting system” also includes the practices and documentation used to:
(i) Identify system components and versions of components;
(ii) Test the system during its development and maintenance;
(iii) Maintain records of system errors and defects;
(iv) Determine specific system changes to be made to a system after the initial qualification of the system; and
(v) Make available any materials to the voter, including without limitation:
(a)
Notices;
(b)
Instructions;
(c)
Forms; or
(d)
Paper ballots.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 70-103. State funding of elections.
(a) The State Board of Election Commissioners is statutorily charged with funding elections designated by law as state-funded using funds appropriated to the State Board of Election Commissioners for election expenses of:
(1) The State Board of Election Commissioners; and
(2) A county board of election commissioners.
(b)(1) State-funded elections include:
(A) Preferential primary elections;
(B) General primary elections;
(C) Special primary elections;
(D) Nonpartisan general elections; and
(E) Statewide special elections.
(2) See Arkansas Code §§ 7-4-101(f)(11) and 7-7-201(a).

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 70-104. Withholding of
state funding.
(a) The State Board of Election Commissioners may withhold funding for eligible state-funded election expenses from any county that fails to comply with the rules developed by the board for the administration of primary elections or fails to comply with applicable state election laws until all requirements are met to the satisfaction of the board, Arkansas Code § 7-7-201(b)(3).
(b)(1) Failure to comply with this part established by the board or failure to deliver to the Secretary of State certified results of the election and any additional election information designated by the Secretary of State may result in withholding of funding for eligible state-funded election expenses to the county until all requirements are met to the satisfaction of the board.
(2) See Arkansas Code §§ 7-5-701(c)(2), 7-4-101(f), 7-4-121(b), election audits, and 7-5-707.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-105. Advance funding

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8214&sectionID=54143. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-105. Advance
funding.
(a) Following each regular legislative session, the State Board of Election Commissioners will establish an estimated average cost per registered voter by county for conducting statewide special elections and for conducting preferential primary elections based upon databases of comparative state-funded election costs maintained by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b) A county is eligible to receive funding from the State Board of Election Commissioners before any statewide special election or preferential primary election upon proper request made to the State Board of Election Commissioners by the county board of election commissioners.
(c) The request must be:
(1) In writing under the signature of all three (3) commissioners; and
(2) Received by the State Board of Election Commissioners at least thirty (30) days before the:
(A) Statewide special election; or
(B) Preferential primary election.
(d) Upon review and approval by staff, the county treasurer will receive funding from the State Board of Election Commissioners based upon:
(1) One-half (1/2) of the county’s previously estimated cost per registered voter for conducting the election; or
(2) A minimum of two thousand five hundred dollars ($2,500) for a statewide special election or five thousand dollars ($5,000) for a preferential primary election, whichever is greater.
(e) The respective county will:
(1) Disburse the funds allocated for election expenses; and
(2) Establish a separate expense code to track and monitor payments made with state funds.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-106. Funding of eligible expenses

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8214&sectionID=54144. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-106. Funding of
eligible expenses.
(a) Before each state-funded election, the State Board of Election Commissioners will furnish each county board of election commissioners with comprehensive guidelines, instructions, and worksheets for requesting payment from the State Board of Election Commissioners for eligible state-funded election expenses.
(b) After each state-funded election, the county board of election commissioners must submit to the State Board of Election Commissioners copies of invoices, receipts, and all other required documentation in support of eligible election expenses incurred in conducting the state-funded election.
(c)(1) A county is eligible to receive funding from the State Board of Election Commissioners after the state-funded election upon proper request made to the State Board of Election Commissioners by the county board of election commissioners.
(2) The county board of election commissioners may designate one (1) or more persons to submit its county’s request for reimbursement to the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(d) A county must follow all instructions and submit its request according to State Board of Election Commissioners rules and guidelines to maximize receipt of funding for eligible state-funded election expenses from the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(e)(1) Upon review by staff and approval by the State Board of Election Commissioners, each county treasurer will receive funding from the State Board of Election Commissioners for eligible state-funded election expenses as defined by this part.
(2) The respective county will:
(A) Disburse the funds allocated for election expenses; and
(B) Establish a separate expense code to track and monitor payments made with state funds.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 70-107. Uniform and
consistent funding.
This part is designed to maintain a high level of fiscal accountability in election administration by:
(1) Providing the uniform and consistent distribution of funds for the reimbursement of eligible expenses from the State Board of Election Commissioners to the counties;
(2) Requiring compliance with election laws and procedures to be eligible for reimbursement; and
(3) Providing information needed for the development of databases of comparative election costs.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-201. Poll workers’ pay

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8215&sectionID=54146. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-201. Poll
workers’ pay.
Poll workers’ pay is reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1) Eligible poll worker pay includes funding for poll workers:
(A) Working polling sites on election day;
(B) Working offsite early voting polling sites; and
(C) Who attend training as specified under separate rule;
(2) A county is eligible to receive funding at the current federal or state minimum wage, whichever is greater at the time of the state-funded election, for the actual number of hours worked up to a maximum of fourteen (14) hours by a poll worker at an election day polling location;
(3) In addition to the poll worker pay, a county is eligible to receive a maximum twenty-five-dollar payment for one (1) election judge per poll on election day;
(4) A county is eligible to receive the current federal or state minimum wage, whichever is greater at the time of the state-funded election, and applicable overtime wages if required by law for the actual number of hours worked by a poll worker:
(A) At an offsite early voting polling site outside of the county clerk’s office; and
(B) Under the direction and supervision of the county board of election commissioners;
(5)(A) A county is eligible to receive an additional one-time payment per poll worker who:
(i) Attends training conducted by a State Board of Election Commissioners-certified trainer; and
(ii) Who works the preferential primary election immediately following the training.
(B) This payment shall be governed by the Rules for Poll Worker and County Clerk Training, 7 CAR pt. 81.
(C) However, the payment may be included in the disbursement paid under this part; and
(6) A county is eligible to receive funding for an average of six (6) eligible poll workers per precinct per state-funded election.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-202. Extra help pay

7 CAR § 70-202. Extra
help pay.
Extra help pay is reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions.
(1) Eligible extra help pay includes funding for:
(A) Workers designated by the county board of election commissioners to prepare, package, and deliver election materials to poll workers for each polling site;
(B) Poll workers designated to return election materials to the county board of election commissioners after the closing of the poll;
(C) Workers to unpack and store election materials after the election;
(D) Election clerks processing absentee ballots; and
(E) Election officials designated to tabulate the vote; and
(2) A county is eligible to receive funding for eligible extra help at the current federal or state minimum wage, whichever is greater at the time of the state-funded election, for the actual number of hours worked.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-203. Extra deputy pay

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8215&sectionID=54148. 7 CAR § 70-203. Extra
deputy pay.
Extra deputy pay is reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1) A county is eligible to receive funding for one (1) extra deputy to the county clerk for the purpose of carrying out the requirements of absentee and early voting at the current federal or state minimum wage, whichever is greater at the time of the state-funded election, and applicable overtime wages if required by law, for the actual number of hours worked per day up to the maximum number of hours per day allowable by law, for a period not to exceed thirty-five (35) days per state-funded election; and
(2) A county with two (2) county seats may receive funding for one (1) additional deputy county clerk subject to the same limitations in the maximum number of hours.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8215&sectionID=54149. 7 CAR § 70-204. Mileage
expenses.
Mileage expenses are reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1) Eligible mileage expenses include funding for the mileage of personnel designated by the county board of election commissioners to deliver election materials to poll workers for each polling site and funding for poll workers designated by the county board of election commissioners to return election materials to the county board of election commissioners after the closing of the poll at the same mileage rate prescribed for state employees in state travel rules, Arkansas Code § 7-4-112(b); and
(2) A county may pay up to two (2) poll workers per poll to return ballots to the central count location when a poll is closed.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-205. Election commissioners’ pay

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8215&sectionID=54150. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-205. Election
commissioners’ pay.
Election commissioners’ pay is reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1) A county is eligible to receive funding for election commissioners’ pay of one hundred twenty-five dollars ($125) per public meeting attended when official business is conducted, up to a maximum of ten (10) public meetings per commissioner per state-funded election;
(2) Public meetings as defined under the Freedom of Information Act of 1967, Arkansas Code § 25-19-101 et seq., include but are not limited to:
(A) Drawing of ballot position;
(B) Certification of ballots;
(C) Selection or alteration of location or boundaries of precincts or polling sites;
(D) Designation of election officials;
(E) Correction of errors or omissions of ballots;
(F) Canvassing and certification of election results;
(G) Canvassing and certification of a recount; and
(H) Election day; and
(3)(A) A county is eligible to receive funding for the hourly wages of a county election commissioner if:
(i) The county board has not voted to appoint a county employee as an election coordinator;
(ii) The wages of an individual election commission do not exceed forty (40) hours in the state-funded election being reimbursed;
(iii) The rate of compensation does not exceed fifteen dollars ($15.00) per hour; and
(iv) The hours would otherwise be reimbursable as extra help or contract labor under this part.
(B) A county may pay a county board member hourly wages in excess of those eligible for reimbursement under this part.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-206. Supply costs

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8215&sectionID=54151. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-206. Supply
costs.
Supply costs are reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1) Eligible supply costs include funding for disposable supplies such as:
(A) Pens;
(B) Pencils;
(C) Pads;
(D) Tape;
(E) Magnifying sheets/glasses;
(F) Spools of string for marking electioneering areas;
(G) Ballot marking instruments/devices;
(H) Envelopes;
(I) Ballot boxes;
(J) Seals;
(K) Stub boxes;
(L) Election kits;
(M) Paper;
(N) Postage for:
(i) Mailing absentee application requests and ballots; and
(ii) Official notice to poll workers and polling sites; and
(O) Paper products necessary for the operation of the voting equipment;
(2) A county may only receive funding for quantities applicable to the specific state-funded election for which it seeks funding; and
(3) In a county in which the county board conducts early voting or maintains vote centers, the county is eligible to receive reimbursement for the cost of internet service necessary to support the poll tablet system subject to the following limitations:
(A) In the event that the county maintains year-round internet service for its elections, the reimbursable cost is limited to the payment for the time period in which the state-funded election occurred; and
(B) The cost of internet services in a single election shall not exceed forty dollars ($40.00) per the combined number of:
(i) Polling sites;
(ii) Vote centers; and
(iii) Offsite early voting locations.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-207. Programming costs

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8215&sectionID=54152. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-207. Programming costs.
Programming costs are reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1)(A) Eligible programming costs include funding for:
(i) Programming and testing of voting machines and electronic vote tabulating devices for state-funded elections;
(ii) Technical support; and
(iii) Labor, mileage, and truck rental, if necessary, for transportation of voting machines and precinct tabulating devices to and from polling sites.
(B) See Arkansas Code §§ 7-5-301(m)(2), 7-5-515(a) and (b), and 7-5-611(a)(1);
(2) A county is eligible to receive a maximum twenty-five dollars ($25.00) per hour for testing:
(A) Voting machines; and
(B) Electronic vote tabulating devices;
(3) A county is eligible to receive a maximum one thousand eighty dollars ($1,080) per state-funded election for contracted technical support authorized under Arkansas Code § 7-5-301(g); and
(4) A county is eligible to receive the current federal or state minimum wage, whichever is greater at the time of the state-funded election, for:
(A) The actual number of hours worked by extra help designated by the county board of election commissioners to transport voting machines and electronic vote tabulating devices to and from the polling sites; plus
(B) Truck rental fees or mileage expenses at the same mileage rate prescribed for state employees in state travel rules.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 70-208. Ballot printing costs

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=1302&subPartID=8215&sectionID=54153. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-208. Ballot
printing costs.
Ballot printing costs are reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1) Counties are eligible to receive funding for printing a number of absentee ballots, provisional ballots, and emergency ballots not to exceed the greater of:
(A) The thirty-five (35) ballots per ballot style; or
(B) One and one-half (1.5) times the number of absentee voters and provisional voters at the last preceding comparable election;
(2) A county using ballot marking devices is eligible to receive funding for the purchase of ballot stock equivalent to one and one-half (1.5) times the number of electors voting on paper ballots at the last preceding comparable election, up to a maximum of one hundred five percent (105%) of the total number of registered voters;
(3) A county using preprinted paper ballots counted by electronic vote tabulating devices is eligible to receive funding for printing a number of ballots equivalent to one and one-half (1.5) times the number of electors voting on paper ballots at the last preceding comparable election, up to a maximum of one hundred five percent (105%) of the total number of registered voters;
(4) In a state-funded primary election in which a county is using preprinted ballots counted by electronic vote tabulating devices, a county is eligible to receive additional funding for printing a number of separate nonpartisan/special election ballots for the nonpartisan general election and any special election that is held on the same dates, times, and places as the preferential primary election equivalent to one and one-half (1.5) times the number of electors voting a separate nonpartisan/special election paper ballot at the last preceding comparable election;
(5) A county is eligible to receive funding for printing a number of special runoff ballots equivalent to a maximum of one and one-half (1.5) times the total number of overseas absentee ballot requests received at the last preceding comparable election or one and one-half (1.5) times the total number of overseas absentee voters voting on paper ballots at the last preceding comparable election, whichever is greater;
(6) A county is eligible to receive funding for printing sample ballots required by law; and
(7)(A) Beginning with the 2026 election cycle, these calculations shall be based on the vote totals reported in the ballot disposition report for the election in question.
(B) Reimbursement shall not be made for expenses governed by this subsection unless a ballot disposition report is on file with the State Board of Election Commissioners.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 70-209. Legal
advertising costs.
Legal advertising costs are reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1) Eligible legal advertising costs include:
(A) One (1) publication of the notice of the meeting in which the order of the candidates on the ballot is determined as required under Arkansas Code §§ 7-5-207 and 7-7-305;
(B) Two (2) publications of the notice of election required under Arkansas Code § 7-5-202;
(C) One (1) publication of the notice that voting machines will be prepared as required under Arkansas Code § 7-5-516;
(D) One (1) publication of the notice of the public test of the voting equipment as required under Arkansas Code § 7-5-515;
(E) One (1) publication of the notice of the tabulation device testing as required under Arkansas Code § 7-5-611; and
(F) One (1) publication of the calling document for statewide special elections and special primary elections; and
(2) A county must provide a copy of the publication proof provided by the publisher to be eligible to receive reimbursement for eligible advertising costs.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 70-210. Facility
fees.
Facility fees are reimbursed pursuant to the following provisions:
(1)(A) Eligible facility fees include fees charged to a county by a facility to offset the cost of using the facility as an election day or offsite early voting polling site for a state-funded election.
(B) A county is eligible to receive a maximum of forty dollars ($40.00) per each facility that requests payment from the county for its use as an election day polling site;
(2) A county is eligible to receive forty dollars ($40.00) per day per each facility that requests payment from the county for its use as an offsite early voting polling site, up to a maximum of four thousand dollars ($4,000) per county per state-funded election; and
(3) A county must provide the State Board of Election Commissioners with a copy of an invoice from each facility to be eligible to receive funding from the board.

7 CAR § 70-211. Ineligible
state-funded election expenses.
(a) Poll workers’ pay reimbursements are limited by the following provisions:
(1) The State Board of Election Commissioners will not provide funding for a volunteer election page serving as a poll worker on election day; and
(2) The board will not provide funding for poll workers who have not received the required training coordinated by the board within twelve (12) months of the date of the state-funded election.
(b) County employee pay reimbursements are limited by the following provisions:
(1) The board will not provide funding to reimburse wages or salary of a permanent full-time or permanent part-time county employee; and
(2) An individual is considered a permanent part-time county employee under this section if the individual:
(A) Works more than five hundred (500) hours in a single election; or
(B) Is considered a permanent part-time employee by the county.
(c) Extra help pay reimbursements are limited by the following provisions:
(1) The total hours reimbursed for the preparation of election materials shall not exceed:
(A) Nine hundred (900) hours in a county with over one hundred thousand (100,000) registered voters;
(B) Six hundred (600) hours in a county with between one hundred thousand (100,000) and fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters; and
(C) Three hundred (300) hours in a county with fewer than fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters;
(2) The total hours reimbursed for the unpacking and storage of election materials shall not exceed:
(A) Four hundred (400) hours in a county with over one hundred thousand (100,000) registered voters;
(B) Two hundred (200) hours in a county with between one hundred thousand (100,000) and fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters; and
(C) One hundred (100) hours in a county with fewer than fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters;
(3) The total hours reimbursed for contract labor related to the testing of voting equipment shall not exceed:
(A) Three hundred (300) hours in a county with over one hundred thousand (100,000) registered voters;
(B) One hundred fifty (150) hours in a county with between one hundred thousand (100,000) and fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters; and
(C) One hundred (100) hours in a county with fewer than fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters;
(4) The total hours reimbursed for contract labor to transport voting equipment shall not exceed:
(A) Five hundred (500) hours in a county with over one hundred thousand (100,000) registered voters;
(B) Two hundred (200) hours in a county with between one hundred thousand (100,000) and fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters; and
(C) One hundred (100) hours in a county with fewer than fifty thousand (50,000) registered voters; and
(5) The hourly wage costs associated with counting ballots shall not be reimbursed by the board if the quorum court of the county in question has voted to hand-count ballots pursuant to Arkansas Code § 7-5-301(c)(1).
(d) Mileage expenses will not be reimbursed by the board if the expenditure is incurred by:
(1) A county election commissioner for travel to and from public meetings; or
(2) A poll worker’s travel to and from the polling site, except as provided by law for delivery of election materials and voting equipment.
(e) Election commissioners’ pay
expenses will not be reimbursed except as provided for in 7 CAR § 70-107 of this part
.
(f) Supply cost reimbursements are limited by the following provisions:
(1) The board will not provide funding for capital or leasehold improvements to polling sites.
(2) The board will not provide funding for nonexpendable equipment and supplies such as:
(A) Voting booths;
(B) Electric pencil sharpeners;
(C) Thermal binding machines;
(D) Pagers;
(E) Outlet strips;
(F) Extension cords;
(G) Adapters;
(H) Phone cords; and
(I) Expensive metal signage.
(3) The board will not provide funding for expendable supplies such as:
(A) Batteries;
(B) Copier toner;
(C) Printer toner cartridges;
(D) Printer ribbons;
(E) Hanging file folders;
(F) Manila file folders;
(G) Binders;
(H) “I Voted” stickers; and
(I) Flags.
(4) The board will not provide funding for the cost:
(A) Of binding voter books;
(B) For reproduction of poll worker training material;
(C) For phone lines or phone services;
(D) For portable toilet rental;
(E) For furniture rentals;
(F) For return postage; or
(G) For food.
(g) Voting system costs will not be reimbursed by the board if the expenditure is described below:
(1) The purchase of new or used voting systems; or
(2) The leasing, rental, maintenance, or depreciation of voting systems.
(h) Programming costs reimbursements are limited by the following provisions:
(1) The board will not provide funding for:
(A) Reprogramming due to county error;
(B) Reprogramming due to vendor error; or
(C) Programming of duplicative ballot styles which cause the county to pay for more than fifty percent (50%) more ballot styles than the election requires; and
(2) The board will not reimburse the additional costs of nonpartisan ballots under 7 CAR § 70-208(4) unless the number of nonpartisan ballots cast in the prior preferential primary is documented on the reimbursement request.
(i) Ballot printing costs reimbursements are limited by the following provisions:
(1) The board will not provide funding for ballot printing reruns due to county or vendor error;
(2) The board will not provide funding for overages in ordering and printing ballots, except by prior written request to the board with prior written approval by the board;
(3) The county or the vendor will be financially responsible for excessive printing of paper ballots; and
(4) The cost associated with producing preprinted ballots shall not be reimbursed by the board if the quorum court of the county in question has voted to hand-count ballots pursuant to Arkansas Code § 7-5-301(c)(1).
(j) Legal advertising costs will not be reimbursed by the board if the expenditure is described below:
(1) Advertising the list of appointed election officials;
(2) Advertising for poll worker training;
(3) Advertising in excess of that required by law as defined previously;
(4) Readvertising due to county error;
(5) Colored advertising; or
(6) Costs related to advertising on:
(A) Radio;
(B) Television broadcasting; or
(C) Social media.
(k) Facility fees reimbursements are limited in that the board will not provide funding for reimbursing individuals hired to clean facilities used as polling sites.
(l) Other reimbursements are limited by the following provisions:
(1) The board will not provide funding for any penalties assessed to a county by any vendor, such as a surcharge assessed to a county by a vendor for missing deadlines established by the vendor.
(2) The board will not consider requests for advance funding for state-funded:
(A) General primary (runoff) elections; or
(B) Special primary elections.
(3) The board will review all shipping and handling fees in excess of five hundred dollars ($500) for consideration of payment on a case-by-case basis.
(4) County funds must be used for funding any election-related expenses incurred by the county in conducting a state-funded election that fall outside the scope of funding by the board as defined by this part.
(5) A county may appeal to the board for reconsideration of any otherwise eligible expense that is disqualified by the board for exceeding maximums established by this part.
(6) If a court orders a new election as a result of a contested election, the board will consider the criteria for funding the expenses of the new court-ordered election on a case-by-case basis.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 71-101.
Scope.
(a) This part will set forth the procedures for calculation, allocation, and billing of the cost of an annual school election that is held on an even-numbered year.
(b) This part will also establish procedures for conducting the annual school election across multiple counties.
(c) This part is in addition to, and not a substitute for, the laws of the State of Arkansas.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-102. Definitions

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28183. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-102.
Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Actual election expense” means the total amount of liabilities or expenditures incurred by a county for a particular election expense in a given election;
(2) “Additional cost” means a cost that:
(A) Is incurred by the county when holding a school election or its runoff in conjunction with a:
(i) Preferential primary election;
(ii) General primary election;
(iii) General election; or
(iv) General election runoff; and
(B) Would not have been incurred had the school election not been held on that date;
(3) “Allocated previous cost” means the portion of the total previous cost that is assigned to each county based on the percentage of the school district’s qualified electors living in each county;
(4) “Annual school election” means the election held once in a calendar year by which the residents in a given school district cast votes to:
(A) Determine which candidates may fill any vacant seat on that district’s board of directors; and
(B) Unless otherwise provided for in law, approve or disapprove the rate of tax proposed by the board of directors;
(5) “County board” refers to the county board of election commissioners;
(6) “Election expense” means any expenditure lawfully used by the county board of election commissioners, the county clerk, or any other authorized person to prepare for, conduct, or determine the results of an election;
(7) “Reimbursable additional cost” means the total cost for which the school district is required to reimburse the county board of election commissioners for the additional cost of a school election that does not exceed the previous cost of the most recent comparable election as defined by this part;
(8) “State-funded elections” means:
(A) Preferential primary elections;
(B) General primary elections;
(C) Special primary elections;
(D) Nonpartisan general elections;
(E) Statewide special elections; and
(F) Runoffs for statewide special elections; and
(9) “Total previous cost” means the cost:
(A) Of the most recent comparable election that was held in an odd-numbered year; and
(B) That the reimbursable additional cost of the election cannot exceed pursuant to Arkansas Code § 6-14-118(b)(2).

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-103. Reimbursement of state-funded elections held in conjunction with annual school election

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28184. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-103.
Reimbursement of state-funded elections held in conjunction with annual school election.
(a) The additional costs incurred by a county for an annual school election that is held in conjunction with a state-funded election are not reimbursable by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b)(1) Any request for reimbursement filed pursuant to the Rules for Reimbursement of Expenses for State-Funded Elections, 7 CAR pt. 70, that requests that the additional cost of a school election be included in that reimbursement or fails to document the calculation required by 7 CAR § 71-108 may result in the withholding of state funding pursuant to 7 CAR § 70-104.
(2) A county submitting a request for reimbursement must calculate the additional cost of the school election as defined under this part.
(3) The requesting county must document those calculations in a manner sufficient for the board to distinguish the:
(A) Additional cost of the school election; and
(B) Reimbursable costs of the state-funded election.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 71-103(a) as follows: "Ark. Code Ann. § 7-4-101(f)(11)".

Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-104. School district’s obligation to reimburse county for school election expenses when election is held in conjunction with another election

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28185. 7 CAR § 71-104.
School district’s obligation to reimburse county for school election expenses when election is held in conjunction with another election.
(a) When an annual school election is held in an even-numbered year, the school district must reimburse the additional election costs created by holding the school election at the same time as the preferential primary or general election to the county or counties that incurred these costs.
(b) However, the amount that is reimbursable to the county or counties shall not exceed an amount equal to the amount the school district reimbursed the county in which the district is domiciled for the last contested school election in an odd-numbered year.
(c) If the total amount of reimbursements requested from all counties under this part exceeds the total previous cost of the school election, the total amount of reimbursements for the additional cost of the election shall be prorated between each county in which an issue or candidate for that district appeared pursuant to 7 CAR § 71-109.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 71-104(a) as follows: "Ark. Code Ann. § 6-14-118 (b)(1) as amended by Act 910 of 2016".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 71-104(b) as follows: "Ark. Code Ann. § 6-14-118 (b)(2) as amended by Act 910 of 2016".
Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-105. Procedures for billing additional election expenses to school district

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28186. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-105.
Procedures for billing additional election expenses to school district.
(a)(1) The county board of election commissioners shall prepare an expense report that documents the total of all additional election expenses incurred by that county for each school district in the county that held an election.
(2) This report shall itemize each election expense for which the county experienced an additional cost in that election.
(3) The county board of election commissioners shall then forward that report to the school district with a request for reimbursement.
(b) This request shall be submitted within one hundred fifty (150) days of the date of the election.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-106. Procedures for school district’s calculation and payment of reimbursements

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28187. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-106.
Procedures for school district’s calculation and payment of reimbursements.
(a) The district shall, pursuant to 7 CAR § 71-109, calculate the previous cost of the most recent comparable odd-year school election for each county in which the district had an issue or candidate on the ballot.
(b) Once the district has received a request for reimbursement from each county board of election commissioners required to submit a request, the district shall pay each county the lesser of the:
(1) Additional election costs requested by the county; or
(2) Allocated previous costs of the most recent comparable election held on an odd year for that county.
(c) In the event that the payment of the lesser of the additional election cost requested by the county or the allocated previous costs to each county does not cause the district’s total reimbursement to exceed the total previous costs, the remaining amount shall be prorated proportionally between those counties whose reimbursement was limited by the allocated previous costs until the district’s reimbursement to all counties, in the aggregate, equals the total previous costs.
(d) Should the district fail to receive a request for reimbursement from one (1) or more counties within one hundred fifty (150) days of the election, the district shall follow the procedures set forth in this part in calculating the payment for each county that has submitted a request at the time the calculations are made.
(e)(1) The district must reimburse each county that has submitted timely requests for reimbursement no later than two hundred ten (210) days following the date of the election.
(2) The district shall only reimburse a county board of election commissioners that files a request after the initial calculations and payment are made if the total reimbursements paid by the district have not yet exceeded the total previous costs.
(f)(1) If, pursuant to this part, the district is not required to pay the entire amount of additional cost requested by the county board of election commissioners, the district shall document:
(A) The amount of additional cost each county submitted to the district;
(B) The election designated as the most recent comparable odd-year election;
(C) The amount the district paid to the domicile county in the comparable odd-year election;
(D) The number of the district’s qualified electors living in each county; and
(E) All calculations made using this information.
(2) A copy of this documentation shall be submitted to each county at the time the district submits its reimbursement to the county.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-107. Calculation of additional costs of school election

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28188. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-107.
Calculation of additional costs of school election.
(a)(1) An additional cost of the election, within the meaning of 7 CAR § 71-105, is calculated as follows.
(2) The county board of election commissioners shall calculate the difference of the actual funds expended or liabilities incurred on any election expense with the amount that would have been required had the annual school election not been held in conjunction with the preferential primary or general election.
(b) Once the county board of election commissioners calculates this difference for each election expense for which there is a difference, the county board of election commissioners shall add the additional cost for each election expenditure together to calculate the total additional cost of the annual school election.
(c)(1) However, notwithstanding any other provision of this part, a county board of election commissioners and a school district may agree that the additional costs of the school election will exceed the reimbursable additional costs.
(2) If such an agreement is reached, the school district may reimburse the county the total reimbursable additional cost as calculated under 7 CAR § 71-109 without calculating the additional costs.
(3)
Note.
A county must still calculate the additional cost of a school election under this part in order to receive reimbursement from the State Board of Election Commissioners in a state-funded election.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-108. Further explanation of additional cost of specific election expenses

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28189. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-108.
Further explanation of additional cost of specific election expenses.
(a)(1) When calculating the additional costs of the school election, the county board of election commissioners shall make its calculation regarding a specific election expense consistent with the procedures described in this part.
(2) However, the following categories are not exclusive and all election expenses are governed by 7 CAR § 71-107.
(3) If more than one (1) district is holding an annual school election within a county on the same election date, the calculations described below shall be performed separately for each district.
(b)
Programming cost — Ballot faces.
(1) When calculating the additional programing cost of formatting ballot styles, the county board of election commissioners shall determine number of additional ballot styles that are required due to the inclusion of issues or races that are to be determined in the annual school election.
(2) The county board of election commissioners shall then multiply that number by the formatting cost for a single ballot style.
(c)
Programming cost — Candidates/issues.
(1) When calculating the additional programing cost of formatting each candidate or issue on the ballot, the county board of election commissioners shall determine number of additional candidates and issues that are required due to the inclusion of issues or races that are to be determined in the annual school election.
(2) The county board of election commissioners shall then multiply that number by the cost of adding a single issue or candidate.
(3) These same calculations shall be applied to the cost of formatting the candidates and issues on the audio ballots.
(d)
Election officials.
When calculating the additional cost of election officials, the county board of election commissioners should only include as additional costs the payment made to election officials appointed during an election solely because of the inclusion of the annual school election.
(e)
Election commissioner meetings.
(1) The county board of election commissioners may include, as an additional cost of the school election, one (1) meeting of the county board of election commissioners to perform the calculation required by this part.
(2) Additional meetings of the county board of election commissioners may only be included if that meeting was called solely to address issues generated by the inclusion of the school election.
(3) However, the county board of election commissioners shall not hold separate meetings to address school election issues solely for the purposes of generating reimbursement under this part.
(f)
Supply cost.
(1) When calculating the additional costs of election supplies, the county board of election commissioners may include as additional costs any additional supplies that are purchased only because of the inclusion of the school election.
(2) Examples of such costs include but are not limited to the cost of extra RTAL rolls due to increased ballot lengths, extra thermal paper, etc.
(g)
Advertising/publication cost.
(1) A portion of the cost of publishing legally required notices can be recovered as an additional cost if the county board of election commissioners is able to determine what portion of the costs are attributed directly to the school election.
(2) If the notice is included in a portion of the publication that is billed using a rate for each word or line of the notice, then the additional cost of the notice is those words or lines used associated with the annual school election.
(h)
Paper ballots.
The additional cost for any paper ballot produced solely due to the inclusion of the annual school election for use on election day, absentee ballot, or provisional ballots shall be an additional cost of the school election.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 71-108(g) as follows: "School Elections have different publication requirements than preferential primary elections.  See A.C.A. §6-14-109; A.C.A. §6-14-106(d)".
"RTAL" means real time audit log.
Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-109. Calculation of previous costs of most recent comparable school election

7 CAR § 71-109.
Calculation of previous costs of most recent comparable school election.
(a) This section governs how the school district board of directors shall calculate the previous cost of the last contested school election in an odd-numbered year.
(1)
Calculation of the most recent odd-numbered year contested election for an at-large board of directors member’s position or a change in the millage rate.
(A) The district shall determine the total amount of reimbursement the school district paid to the county in which the district is domiciled for the most recent odd-year election requiring polling sites to be opened districtwide.
(B) The district shall then determine the percentage of the school district’s qualified electors that reside in each county of the district as of the close of voter registration for that election.
(C) The previous cost of the most recent election for each county in the district shall be the percentage of the total reimbursement in the previous election that corresponds with the percentage of qualified electors living in each county.
(2)
Calculation of the most recent odd-numbered year contested election for board of directors members’ positions elected by zone if no change in the school millage appears on the ballot.
(A) The district shall determine the total amount of reimbursement paid by the district to the county in which the district is domiciled for the most recent odd-year election in which:
(i) The same number of zones were contested as are contested in the current election; and
(ii) Polls were not required to be open districtwide.
(B) The district shall then determine the percentage of the school district’s qualified electors that reside in the zone or zones that are contested in the present elections for each county as of the close of voter registration for that election.
(C) The previous cost of the most recent election for each county in this case shall be the percentage of the total reimbursement in the previous election that corresponds with the percentage of qualified electors living in the contested zones that lie in each county.
(b)(1) If a school district cannot determine the cost of the most recent odd-year election in which the appropriate number of zones were contested, the district shall instead use the most recent contested school district election in which polls were open countywide to calculate previous cost.
(2) The county board of election commissioners for the domiciled county shall assist the district, if requested, in determining the amount of past reimbursements.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-110. Special rules of calculation of annual school election held with general election or general election runoff

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28191. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-110.
Special rules of calculation of annual school election held with general election or general election runoff.
(a)(1) When an annual school election is held at the same time as the general election or its runoff, the county board of election commissioners shall first calculate the additional costs of the annual school election.
(2) That calculation shall be made pursuant to this part and relevant state law.
(b) The county board of election commissioners shall then subtract the additional cost of the school election from the actual election expenses to determine the total cost of the election to the county pursuant to this part.
(c) The county board of election commissioners shall then use this number to calculate the portion of the election costs that municipalities must reimburse to the county pursuant to Arkansas Code § 7-5-104.
(d) The county board of election commissioners shall provide to the appropriate official in each city, or incorporated town within the county, a bill that clearly states:
(1) The amount that city or incorporated town must reimburse the county for the election; and
(2) A concise explanation of how this amount was calculated.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

### 7 CAR § 71-111. Reimbursement of runoff elections in even-numbered years

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28192. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-111.
Reimbursement of runoff elections in even-numbered years.
(a) The entire cost of a runoff in an annual school election held on an even year shall be reimbursed by the school district to the county if the:
(1) Runoff is caused by the school election; and
(2) School election issue is the only issue on the ballot.
(b)(1) If there is a runoff in both the annual school election and another election held with the annual school election, the additional costs of that runoff shall be reimbursed to the county pursuant to this part in the same manner as the annual school election.
(2) The allocated previous cost that is used to calculate the reimbursable additional cost for the runoff election shall be the same as the allocated previous cost used in the calculation for the reimbursement for the annual school election that necessitated the runoff.
(c)(1) This subsection shall apply when a runoff election for the annual school election is held in which:
(A) Some of the polling locations that are opened have only school election issues on the ballot; and
(B) Other polling locations with the same school election runoff also have additional runoff issues from another election on the ballot.
(2) The school district shall reimburse the county for all election costs associated with the polling locations with only the school election runoff on their ballots.
(3) The school district shall reimburse the county for all additional costs associated with those polling locations with multiple runoff issues on the ballot.
(4) The additional cost for these polling locations shall be calculated in the same manner as the annual school election.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 71-111(a) as follows: "Ark. Code Ann. § 6-14-118(c)".
Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 71-112.
Preelection requirements of county clerk’s office for candidate filing.
(a)(1) At the close of candidate filing, the county clerk of the county in which a school district is domiciled shall certify to the board of election commissioners in the domicile county the names of all candidates:
(A) Who have properly filed for office; and
(B) Whose petitions have been accepted.
(2) The county clerk must also certify any write-in candidates who have properly filed.
(b) When an annual school election is held in an even-numbered year, the county clerk of the domicile county shall also certify to the county boards of election commissioners for any other county in which the school district has territory the same list of school board candidates and write-in candidates.
(c) The certified list of candidates shall state:
(1) The zone for which each candidate or write-in candidate is seeking election; and
(2) All counties that lie within that zone.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 71-112(a) as follows: "Ark. Code Ann. § 6-14-111(i)".

Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

Preelection requirements of school district for issues on the ballot.
