### 7 CAR § 71-114. Procedures for production and transfer of election returns in polling locations outside the county in which a school district is domiciled

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=147&partID=539&subPartID=4476&sectionID=28195. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 71-114.
Procedures for production and transfer of election returns in polling locations outside the county in which a school district is domiciled.
(a)(1) At the close of the polls for an election held in an even-numbered year, the election officials in each polling location or central tabulation location in which election results are produced by printing a results tape or other document from a voting machine shall print one (1) additional results tape for each school district that:
(A) Is not domiciled in that county; and
(B) Has a candidate or ballot issue reflected in those returns.
(2) All additional copies of the results tape shall be signed by the appropriate election officials as certified returns.
(3) Election officials of a county that a school district is not domiciled in shall file certified election returns for all precincts voting in that school district’s election with the county clerk of the county in which each school district is domiciled.
(b)(1) If voting results are counted in any other manner, the election official certifying the returns for each polling location or central tabulation location shall produce an additional certified return for each school district that:
(A) Is not domiciled in that county; and
(B) Has a candidate or ballot issue reflected in those returns.
(2) Election officials of a county in which a school district is not domiciled shall file certified election returns for all precincts voting in that school district’s election with the county clerk of the county in which each school district is domiciled.
(c)(1) The county board of election commissioners of any county that is required to file certified school election returns with the county clerk of a neighboring county in which that school district is domiciled is responsible for ensuring that these returns reach the neighboring county clerk and county board of election commissioners on election night.
(2) If for any reason the additional results tapes are not produced and filed with the neighboring county clerk, the county board of election commissioners shall:
(A) Produce a copy of all results from all precincts which included that school district’s races;
(B) Certify the veracity of those results; and
(C) File that copy with the appropriate county clerk and county board of election commissioners.
(d) Returns for the annual school election in even years shall be reported as unofficial election results using the statewide election night reporting system by the county in which the votes were cast.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 71-114(a) as follows: "Ark. Code Ann. § 6-14-115(a)".
Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 6-14-101; Arkansas Code § 7-7-201

7 CAR § 72-102. Definitions.
For purposes of this part:
(1) “Annual salary” means the salary established by the General Assembly during regular sessions or the Independent Citizens Commission for each office that is in effect on the last day of the fiscal year preceding the fiscal year in which the candidate files for office;
(2) “Filing fee” means the fee paid by a candidate for the offices of Justice of the Supreme Court, Judge of the Court of Appeals, circuit judge, district judge, and prosecuting attorney to the Secretary of State at the time of filing his or her political practices pledge; and
(3) “Nonpartisan office” means the offices of:
(A) Justice of the Supreme Court;
(B) Judge of the Court of Appeals;
(C) Circuit judge;
(D) District judge; or
(E) Prosecuting attorney.

Codification Notes:
This part as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 72-102(b) following the phrase "or prosecuting attorney" as follows: "A.C.A. §§ 7-1-101(24)(A); 7-10-102(a)".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-10-103

A candidate for a nonpartisan office may access the ballot by:
(1) Paying a filing fee;
(2) Filing a petition; or
(3) Filing as a write-in candidate in the manner provided under Arkansas Code § 7-10-103.

7 CAR § 72-106. Nonpartisan filing fee fund.
(a) The filing fees collected for the offices of Justice of the Supreme Court, Judge of the Court of Appeals, circuit judge, and district judge shall be remitted to the Treasurer of State for deposit into the Nonpartisan Filing Fee Fund for covering the cost of election expenses of the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b)(1) The first one hundred thousand dollars ($100,000) collected annually from filing fees for the office of prosecuting attorney shall be remitted to the Treasurer of State for deposit into the Trial Court Administrator Fund.
(2) The remaining filing fees collected for the office of prosecuting attorney shall be remitted to the Treasurer of State for deposit in the Nonpartisan Filing Fee Fund.

7 CAR § 72-107. Filing fees.
(a) The filing fee for the Chief Justice of the Supreme Court shall be six percent (6%) of the annual salary.
(b) The filing fee for Associate Justice of the Supreme Court shall be six percent (6%) of the annual salary.
(c) The filing fee for Judge of the Court of Appeals shall be five percent (5%) of the annual salary.
(d) The filing fee for circuit judge shall be four percent (4%) of the annual salary.
(e) The filing fee for prosecuting attorney shall be three percent (3%) of the annual salary.
(f) The filing fee for district judge shall be three percent (3%) of the annual salary.
(g) All filing fees calculated under this section shall be rounded up to the next ten dollars ($10.00).
(h) The filing fees for each office shall be converted into dollar amounts pursuant to this part and published in the “Running for Public Office Handbook” by the State Board of Election Commissioners.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-10-103

### 7 CAR § 80-102. Definitions

7 CAR § 80-102.
Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Candidate” means any individual who has knowingly and willingly taken affirmative action, including solicitation of funds, for the purpose of seeking nomination for or election to any public office;
(2) “County board of election commissioners” means the three-member board in each of the seventy-five (75) counties in this state responsible for conducting all elections within its respective county, including:
(A) Two (2) members elected by the county committee of the majority party and one (1) member elected by the county committee of the minority party; or
(B) Persons appointed by the state chair of either the majority or minority party to fill a vacancy in that party’s position; and
(3)(A) “Election official” means a person:
(i) Who is a member of the county board of election commissioners;
(ii) Who performs election coordinator duties;
(iii) Who is a poll worker designated by a county board of election commissioners to be:
(a)
An election clerk;
(b)
An election judge;
(c)
An election sheriff; or
(d)
A deputy county clerk; or
(iv) Assigned by a county clerk to conduct early voting.
(B) “Election official” means a person:
(i) Who serves as a member of the county board of election commissioners;
(ii) Who is appointed by the county board of election commissioners to serve as:
(a)
An election coordinator;
(b)
A person appointed to perform tasks related to the election that require the handling of:
(1)
Ballots; or
(2)
Other election materials or equipment;
(c)
An election clerk;
(d)
An election judge;
(e)
An election sheriff; or
(f)
An absentee ballot clerk; or
(iii) Assigned by a county clerk to conduct early voting administered by the county clerk.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 80-102(2)(B) cited "1 A.C.A. § 7-4-102(a)".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 80-102(1) cited "2 A.C.A. § 7-6-201(2)".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 80-103. Qualifications of a member of the county board of election commissioners

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=541&subPartID=4572&sectionID=28767. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 80-103.
Qualifications of a member of the county board of election commissioners.
(a) The qualifications of a member of the county board of election commissioners as determined by the General Assembly are as follows:
(1) Must be a qualified elector of this state;
(2) Must be able to read and write the English language;
(3) Must be a resident of the county in which he or she serves at the time of his or her appointment or election;
(4) Must not have been found guilty or pled guilty or nolo contendere to the violation of any criminal election law of this state;
(5) Must not be a paid employee of any political party;
(6) Must not be a paid employee of any person running for any office on the county’s ballot;
(7) Must not participate in the campaign of any candidate listed on a ballot or of a write-in candidate seeking election in that county that falls under their jurisdiction or authority;
(8) Must not manage a campaign;
(9) Must not perform labor for a campaign;
(10) Must not solicit on behalf of a candidate or campaign;
(11) Must not pass out handbills, signs, or other literature concerning a candidate’s campaign;
(12) Must not assist a candidate’s campaign at a rally or parade;
(13) Must not display candidate placards or signs on an automobile;
(14) Must not be employed with a company that has any business dealings, contracts, or pending contracts with the county board of election commissioners at the time of his or her appointment to the county board of election commissioners;
(15) Must not be a candidate for any office to be filled at an election while serving on the county board of election commissioners, except for appearing on the ballot as a candidate for a position in his or her political party;
(16) Must not be a chair or secretary of a county political party;
(17) Must not be married to or related within the second degree of consanguinity to any candidate running for office in the current election if objection to the service is made to the county board of election commissioners within seven (7) calendar days after the posting of the list of officials; and
(18) Must not be the spouse of a chair of a county political party or the spouse of another county board of election commissioner if objection to the service is made to the county board of election commissioners within ten (10) calendar days after the posting of the list of officials.
(b) If an objection is filed against married members of the county election commission, the objection can only be filed against one (1) of the two (2) married individuals.
(c) A member of the county board of election commissioners may:
(1) Make a financial contribution to a candidate;
(2) Attend a political party’s state, district, or county meeting where a candidate or issue advocate speaks as a member of the audience; or
(3) Participate in a political party convention.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained footnotes to the following: A footnote following the phrase "The qualifications" cited "4 A.C.A. § 7-4-109 as amended by Act 1253 of 2015".
A footnote following the phrase "General Assembly" cited "5 Art 3, § 10 of the Arkansas Constitution, as amended by Amendment 85".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 80-103(c)(3) cited "6 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(c)(2)(B)(ii) as amended by Act 1253 of 2015".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 80-104. Required training

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=541&subPartID=4572&sectionID=28768. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 80-104.
Required training.
(a) Each member of the county boards of election commissioners shall attend:
(1) Either a comprehensive training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners before the regularly scheduled preferential primary election; or
(2) An advanced training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b)(1) Advanced training will be made available if the Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners determines that, based on the amount of changes in law and procedure from the previous election cycle, an advanced training program is appropriate for experienced county commissioners.
(2) Advanced trainings:
(A) Are to only be utilized for commissioners who are unable to attend the comprehensive training; and
(B) May be conducted remotely.
(3) Advanced trainings may not necessarily include a detailed review of all basic laws and procedures that are unchanged from the previous two (2) election cycles but will address all aspects of the duties of an election commissioner.
(c) Any member of a county board of election commissioners who has not served on the commission during the previous two (2) preferential primary and general elections and previously attended the comprehensive training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners must attend the comprehensive training program.
(d) A member of a county board of election commissioners shall attend additional interim training if deemed necessary and appropriate by the State Board of Election Commissioners.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 80-104(b)(3) as follows: "7 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(e)(1)".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

7 CAR § 80-105.
Examination and certification of election commissioners.
(a) Having attended the instructional portion of the training program, the election commissioner shall take an examination of essential skills developed and administered by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b) The board shall issue a certification to each commissioner who:
(1) Attended the instructional program; and
(2) Demonstrated competency in the skills essential for conducting an election through the examination process.
(c) Certification shall be issued by the board to each designated trainee who is certified though the training program.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 80-106. Compensation

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=541&subPartID=4572&sectionID=28770. 7 CAR § 80-106.
Compensation.
(a) Upon completion of training by the State Board of Election Commissioners, each county election commissioner attending the training is eligible to receive mileage reimbursement at the rate established for state employees by state travel rules.
(b) Any election commissioner who is trained and certified under this section and who remains in office until the certification of the preferential primary election results shall be eligible to receive three hundred dollars ($300) in compensation for attending training.
(c) If the county political party elects a new commissioner following the preferential primary, the commissioner shall be eligible to receive mileage for additional required trainings only but is not eligible to receive additional compensation.
(d)(1) All compensation and reimbursement paid under this section shall:
(A) Be paid to the county treasurer; and
(B) Include a statement explaining:
(i) The purpose of the funds; and
(ii) Who is entitled to receive the funds under state law.
(2) Mileage for training shall be paid upon:
(A) Receipt of the reporting form approved by the board; and
(B) Verification that the commissioner was tested.
(3) Payment of the three hundred dollars ($300) shall be paid upon verification by the Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners that the commissioner who attended training remains an election commissioner for the county at the time the preferential primary is certified.
(e) Attendance by county election coordinators is optional, and compensation for attending training will not be provided by the board.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 80-106(a) as follows: "8 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(e)(2)".
Authority:

Noncompliance.
The State Board of Election Commissioners may withhold funding for county election commissioner training attendance from any county that fails to comply with the rules and guidelines developed by the board for the administration of election commissioner training until all requirements are met to the satisfaction of the board.

7 CAR § 81-101.
Scope.
(a) This part will set forth the methods for coordinating training on early voting and election-day procedures for poll workers and county clerks in the seventy-five (75) counties in the state.
(b) This part is in addition to, and not a substitute for, the laws of the State of Arkansas.

Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

7 CAR § 81-102.
Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Advanced poll worker training” means the curriculum developed and approved by the State Board of Election Commissioners that poll judges and poll sheriffs are required to complete;
(2) “Basic poll worker training” means the curriculum developed and approved by the State Board of Election Commissioners that all poll workers are required to complete;
(3)(A) “Election official” means a person:
(i) Who is a member of the county board of election commissioners;
(ii) Who performs election coordinator duties;
(iii) Who is a poll worker designated by a county board of election commissioners to be:
(a)
An election clerk;
(b)
An election judge;
(c)
An election sheriff; or
(d)
A deputy county clerk; or
(iv) Assigned by a county clerk to conduct early voting.
(B) “Election official” includes a person:
(i) Who serves as a member of the county board of election commissioners;
(ii) Who is appointed by the county board of election commissioners to serve as:
(a)
An election coordinator;
(b)
A person appointed to perform tasks related to the election that require the handling of:
(1)
Ballots; or
(2)
Other election materials or equipment;
(c)
An election clerk;
(d)
An election judge;
(e)
An election sheriff; or
(f)
An absentee ballot clerk; or
(iii) Assigned by a county clerk to conduct early voting administered by the county clerk;
(4) “Poll worker” means an election official designated by the:
(A) County board of election commissioners to be an election clerk, election judge, or election sheriff at a polling site; or
(B) County clerk to be an election clerk, election judge, or election sheriff at an early voting location;
(5) “Polling site” means a location selected by the county board of election commissioners where votes are cast; and
(6) “Precinct” means a geographical area, the boundaries of which are determined by a county board of election commissioners to facilitate voting by the registered voters from that geographical area.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 81-103. Qualifications of election officials designated as poll workers

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=548&subPartID=4477&sectionID=28198. 7 CAR § 81-103.
Qualifications of election officials designated as poll workers.
The qualifications determined by the General Assembly of an election official designated by the county board of election commissioners to serve as a poll worker are as follows:
(1) Must be a qualified elector of this state;
(2) Must be able to read and write the English language;
(3) Must be a resident of a precinct permitted to vote at the polling site in which he or she serves at the time of his or her appointment, unless the county board of election commissioners determines unanimously that it is impossible to obtain qualified poll workers from the precinct, in which case the poll worker shall be a qualified county resident;
(4) Must not have been found guilty or pled guilty or nolo contendere to the violation of any criminal election law of this state;
(5) Must not be a paid employee of any political party;
(6) Must not be a paid employee of any person running for any office on the county’s ballot;
(7) Must not be a candidate for any office to be filled at an election while serving as a poll worker;
(8) Must not be married to or related within the second degree of consanguinity to any candidate running for office in the current election if objection to the service is made to the county board of election commissioners within seven (7) calendar days after the posting of the list of officials;
(9) Must not be the spouse of a member of the county board of election commissioners if objection to the service is made to the county board of election commissioners within ten (10) calendar days after the posting of the list of officials; and
(10) Must not be the chair or the spouse of a chair of a county political party if objection to the service is made to the county board of election commissioners within ten (10) calendar days after the posting of the list of officials.

Codification Notes:

Footnote to 7 CAR § 81-103 following the phrase "General Assembly" cited "2 Art 3, § 10 of the Arkansas Constitution, as amended by Amendment 85".

### 7 CAR § 81-104. Required training for certified poll worker trainers

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=548&subPartID=4477&sectionID=28199. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 81-104.
Required training for certified poll worker trainers.
(a) Each county board of election commissioners shall:
(1)(A) Designate at least two (2) qualified electors of the county to attend training conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners before the regularly scheduled preferential primary election for the purpose of being certified by the State Board of Election Commissioners as poll worker trainers for the county.
(B) Each designee shall attend either:
(i) A comprehensive training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners before the regularly scheduled preferential primary election; or
(ii) An advanced training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners after each regular legislative session.
(C) Any designee who has not served as a certified poll worker trainer during the previous two (2) preferential primary and general elections and previously attended the comprehensive training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners must attend the comprehensive training program; and
(2)(A) Ensure that all poll workers at each preferential primary election polling site for a regularly scheduled election attended election training conducted locally by State Board of Election Commissioners-certified trainers and coordinated by the State Board of Election Commissioners prior to each regularly scheduled preferential primary election.
(B) All poll workers appointed by the county election commission are required to receive the basic poll worker training curriculum approved by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(C) At least two (2) poll workers at each polling location are required to receive the advanced poll worker training curriculum approved by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(D) The county election commission may elect to administer the full training curriculum to all poll workers in the county.
(b) For all elections following the regularly scheduled preferential primary election, county boards of election commissioners shall ensure that each poll worker has attended election training coordinated by the State Board of Election Commissioners within twelve (12) months prior to the election in which they are serving.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 81-104(a)(2)(A) cited "3 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(e)(1)".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 81-104(b) cited "4 A.C.A. § 7-4-107(b)(2)(C) as amended by Act 966 of 2019".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

7 CAR § 81-105.
Certification and compensation of poll worker trainers.
(a) Upon successful completion of training conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners:
(1) Certification, valid for a period of two (2) years, shall be issued by the board to each designated trainee who successfully completes the training program;
(2) A maximum of two (2) certified trainers per county are eligible to receive one hundred dollars ($100) each plus mileage reimbursement at the rate established for state employees by state travel rules;
(3) The two (2) certified trainers per county are eligible to receive an additional fifty dollars ($50) each per training session, up to a maximum of four (4) sessions each, for conducting poll worker training locally before the preferential primary election; and
(4) Certified trainers’ compensation will be paid by the board to the county treasurer upon receipt of the reporting form approved and provided by the board to the county for that purpose.
(b)(1) Certification and compensation of additional county trainers will be considered by the board on a case-by-case basis based upon need and availability of sufficient resources and funding.
(2) The compensation of the certified trainers for more than two (2) training sessions will also be considered by the board on a case-by-case basis based upon need and availability of sufficient resources and funding.
(c) Requests for additional certified trainers or compensation for additional training sessions in excess of the limitation established in this section must:
(1) Be made in writing; and
(2) Describe the facts and circumstances that make the additional personnel and funding necessary for the effective training of county trainers.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 81-106. Examination of poll worker trainers

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=548&subPartID=4477&sectionID=28201. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 81-106.
Examination of poll worker trainers.
(a) In order to complete this training program, the person designated as a poll worker trainer shall complete an examination of essential skills developed and administered by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b) A person who fails to obtain a passing score on this examination shall not:
(1) Be certified as a county poll worker trainer; or
(2) Receive compensation for attending the certified poll worker training.
(c) The board shall issue a certification to each poll worker trainer designee who attended poll worker training and demonstrated competency in the skills essential for conducting an election through the examination process.
(d) A poll worker trainer designee may take the examination up to three (3) times.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 81-107. Examination of poll workers

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=548&subPartID=4477&sectionID=28202. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 81-107.
Examination of poll workers.
(a)(1) Each poll worker trained pursuant to this part shall complete an examination of essential skills:
(A) Developed by the State Board of Election Commissioners; and
(B) Administered by a certified poll worker trainer for the county.
(2)(A) The county board of election commissioners shall issue a certification to each poll worker who:
(i) Attended poll worker training; and
(ii) Demonstrated competency in the skills essential for conducting an election through the examination process.
(B) Poll workers shall take the examination corresponding with the level of training completed by the poll worker.
(C) A poll worker may take the examination up to three (3) times prior to an election in which the poll worker has been appointed to serve.
(3) The certification shall include the date:
(A) The certification is issued; and
(B) One (1) year from issuance upon which the certification expires.
(b) The certified poll worker trainers for the county shall certify to the Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners under penalty of perjury the following information regarding poll worker training they conduct:
(1) The name of each poll worker certified;
(2) The date the poll worker was certified;
(3) The poll worker’s score on the examination of essential skills;
(4) The name of the certified poll worker trainer or trainers administering the training and examination to the poll worker;
(5) The number of election day and CBEC-administered early voting polling sites in the county; and
(6) Whether the poll worker was appointed by the:
(A) County board of election commissioners; or
(B) County clerk.

Codification Notes:
"CBEC" means county board of election commissioners.
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 81-108. Compensation for poll worker training attendance

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=548&subPartID=4477&sectionID=28203. 7 CAR § 81-108.
Compensation for poll worker training attendance.
(a) A certified poll worker who attends the basic poll worker training curriculum administered by a State Board of Election Commissioners-certified poll worker trainer and who works the preferential primary election immediately following the training is eligible to receive a maximum thirty-five-dollar one-time payment for attending training.
(b) A certified poll worker who attends the advanced poll worker training curriculum administered by a board-certified poll worker trainer and who works the preferential primary election immediately following the training is eligible to receive a maximum fifty-five-dollar one-time payment for attending training.
(c) A county is eligible to receive compensation for poll workers attending the basic poll worker training up to a maximum of ten (10) eligible poll workers per polling site per preferential primary election.
(d) A county is eligible to receive compensation for poll workers attending advanced poll worker training up to a maximum of two (2) eligible poll workers per polling site per preferential primary election.
(e) Poll worker compensation will be paid by the board to the county treasurer upon receipt of the information certified to the Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners under 7 CAR § 81-106(b).
(f) Under Arkansas Code § 7-4-109(e)(2)(B), an election official who does not pass an examination of essential skills is not eligible for compensation for attending a board-coordinated training.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 81-108(a) cited "5 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(e)(2)".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 81-110(b) cited "6 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(e)(2)".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 81-109. Required training for county clerks

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=548&subPartID=4477&sectionID=28204. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 81-109.
Required training for county clerks.
(a)(1) A county clerk or his or her designee shall attend training for poll worker trainers conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners before the regularly scheduled preferential primary election.
(2) Each county clerk or designee shall attend:
(A) Either a comprehensive training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners before the regularly scheduled preferential primary election; or
(B) An advanced training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b)(1)(A) Advanced training will be made available if the Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners determines that, based on the amount of changes in law and procedure from the previous election cycle, an advanced training program is appropriate for experienced county clerks.
(B) Advanced trainings:
(i) Are to only be utilized for clerks or designees who are unable to attend the comprehensive training; and
(ii) May be conducted remotely.
(C) Advanced trainings may not include a detailed review of all basic laws and procedures that are unchanged from the previous two (2) election cycles but will address all aspects of the duties of the county clerk’s office in conducting an election.
(2) Any county clerk or designee who has not served as an election official during the previous two (2) preferential primary and general elections and previously attended the comprehensive training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners must attend the comprehensive training program.
(c)(1) A county clerk or a county clerk’s designee may train any poll worker appointed by the county clerk to conduct clerk-administered early voting.
(2) However, a county clerk or a county clerk’s designee may not train election officials appointed by the CBEC unless they or a county clerk’s designee is designated as a certified poll worker trainer by the CBEC.
(d)(1) A county clerk or his or her designee shall be administered the same examination as the county’s certified poll worker trainers.
(2) In the event a county clerk has sent a designee to this training, the director shall notify the county clerk if the designee fails to demonstrate competency in the skills essential for conducting an election through the examination process.
(e) County clerks or clerk designees who attend training under this section:
(1) Are eligible to receive mileage reimbursement at the rate established for state employees by state travel rules; and
(2) Are not eligible to receive any additional compensation.
(f) Any reimbursement will be paid by the State Board of Election Commissioners to the county treasurer upon receipt of the reporting form approved and provided by the State Board of Election Commissioners to the county for that purpose.

Codification Notes:
"CBEC" means county board of election commissioners.
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 81-110. Required training for poll workers in clerk-administered early voting

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=548&subPartID=4477&sectionID=28205. 7 CAR § 81-110. Required training for poll workers in clerk-administered early voting.
(a) The county clerk shall ensure poll workers selected by the county clerk to conduct early voting have been trained by a person authorized to conduct poll worker training pursuant to this part.
(b) The examination of poll workers appointed by the county clerk to conduct early voting administered by the county clerk shall be conducted pursuant to 7 CAR § 81-107.
(c) Poll workers appointed by the county clerk to conduct early voting administered by the county clerk are eligible for compensation pursuant to the requirements of 7 CAR § 81-108.
(d) An election official who is appointed by the county clerk and the county board of election commissioners may not receive more than one (1) compensation payment for attending training under this part.
(e) The county clerk, with the consent of the county board of election commissioners, may:
(1) Allow the certified poll worker trainers appointed by the county board of election commissioners to train the county clerk’s early voting election staff; and
(2) Designate a certified poll worker trainer appointed by the county board of election commissioners as the county clerk’s designee under Arkansas Code § 7-4-109(e)(1)(B).
(f) For all elections following the regularly scheduled preferential primary election, the county clerk shall ensure that each poll worker appointed by the county clerk has attended election training coordinated by the State Board of Election Commissioners within twelve (12) months prior to the election in which they are serving.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 81-110(f) as follows: "7 A.C.A. § 7-4-107(b)(2)(C) as amended by Act 966 of 2019".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

Noncompliance.
The State Board of Election Commissioners may withhold funding for poll worker and county clerk training attendance from any county that fails to comply with the rules and guidelines developed by the board for the administration of poll worker training until all requirements are met to the satisfaction of the board.

7 CAR § 81-112.
Training materials.
(a) The State Board of Election Commissioners will provide training materials for local poll worker training to each of the seventy-five (75) counties.
(b)(1) Poll worker training shall be conducted only by board-certified trainers.
(2) Trainers are required to use materials provided by the board.
(3) This requirement includes the presentation of all multimedia provided by the board.
(c)(1) Any exception to the requirements of this section must be by prior written request to the board with prior written approval by the board.
(2) Approval by the board is not required for materials related to voting system training or supplemental procedures not addressed by materials provided by the board.

(b) This part is in addition to, and not a substitute for, the laws of the State of Arkansas.

### 7 CAR § 82-102. Definitions

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=549&subPartID=4573&sectionID=28774. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 82-102.
Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1) “Candidate” means any individual who has knowingly and willingly taken affirmative action, including solicitation of funds, for the purpose of seeking nomination for or election to any public office;
(2) “County board of election commissioners” means the three-member board in each of the seventy-five (75) counties in this state responsible for conducting all elections within its respective county, including:
(A) Two (2) members elected by the county committee of the majority party and one (1) member elected by the county committee of the minority party; or
(B) Persons appointed by the state chair of either the majority or minority party to fill a vacancy in that party’s position;
(3) “Election coordinator” means the person:
(A) Appointed by majority vote of the county election commission to receive election coordinator training under this rule; and
(B) To whom tasks can be delegated or assigned by the county election commissioners; and
(4)(A) “Election official” means a person:
(i) Who is a member of the county board of election commissioners;
(ii) Who performs election coordinator duties;
(iii) Who is a poll worker designated by a county board of election commissioners to be:
(a)
An election clerk;
(b)
An election judge;
(c)
An election sheriff; or
(d)
A deputy county clerk; or
(iv) Assigned by a county clerk to conduct early voting.
(B) “Election official” means a person:
(i) Who serves as a member of the county board of election commissioners;
(ii) Who is appointed by the county board of election commissioners to serve as:
(a)
An election coordinator;
(b)
A person appointed to perform tasks related to the election that require the handling of:
(1)
Ballots; or
(2)
Other election materials or equipment;
(c)
An election clerk;
(d)
An election judge;
(e)
An election sheriff; or
(f)
An absentee ballot clerk; or
(iii) Assigned by a county clerk to conduct early voting administered by the county clerk.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 82-102(1) cited "[1] A.C.A. § 7-6-201(2)".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 82-102(2)(B) cited "2 A.C.A. § 7-4-102(a)".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 82-103. Eligibility of an election coordinator

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=549&subPartID=4573&sectionID=28775. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 82-103.
Eligibility of an election coordinator.
(a) An election coordinator is defined by state law as an election official.
(b) The General Assembly has defined the minimum standards for eligibility to serve as an election official as follows:
(1) Must be a qualified elector of this state;
(2) Must be able to read and write the English language;
(3) Must not have been found guilty or pled guilty or nolo contendere to the violation of any criminal election law of this state;
(4) Must not be a paid employee of any political party;
(5) Must not be a paid employee of any person running for any office on the county’s ballot;
(6) Must not be a candidate for any office to be filled at an election while serving on the county board of election commissioners, except for appearing on the ballot as a candidate for a position in his or her political party;
(7) Must not be married to or related within the second degree of consanguinity to any candidate running for office in the current election if objection to the service is made within seven (7) calendar days after the posting of the list of officials for a particular election; and
(8) Must not be the spouse of a chair of a county political party or the spouse of a member of the county board of election commissioners if objection to the service is made within ten (10) calendar days after the posting of the list of officials.
(c) The county board of election commissioners may choose any person meeting these qualifications and who is willing to serve in this role, including but not limited to:
(1) An employee of the county;
(2) A member of the county board of election commissioners;
(3) A county clerk;
(4) A deputy clerk or employee of the county clerk; or
(5) Another eligible person willing to serve as an election coordinator.
(d)(1) In the event that the person considered for appointment as an election coordinator is an employee hired and supervised by an elected county official, the elected county official shall agree that the county board of election commissioners shall be the sole entity instructing the employee regarding the fulfillment of election-related duties prior to the coordinator’s appointment.
(2) In the event that an eligible election coordinator cannot be identified, the county election commissioner shall designate a member of the county election commission to attend election coordinator training.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 82-103(a) cited "3 A.C.A. § 7-1-101(11)".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 82-103(b)(6) cited "4 A.C.A. § 7-4-109".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 82-103(b)(8) cited "5 A.C.A. § 7-5-202(a)(2)".
Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

### 7 CAR § 82-104. Appointment of the county’s election coordinator

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=549&subPartID=4573&sectionID=28776. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 82-104.
Appointment of the
county’s election coordinator.
(a) The county board of election commissioners shall appoint one (1) person meeting the requirements of 7 CAR § 82-103 to serve as the county’s election coordinator.
(b) The county board of election commissioners shall appoint an election coordinator no later than September 1 of the year preceding the year in which the preferential primary is held.
(c) The county board of election commissioners shall certify the person appointed to serve as the coordinator to the State Board of Election Commissioners in writing no later than October 1 of the year preceding the year in which the preferential primary is held.
(d) In the event that an election coordinator’s office becomes vacant, the county board of election commissioners shall:
(1) Notify the State Board of Election Commissioners when a vacancy occurs;
(2) Appoint a new election coordinator within thirty (30) days of the vacancy; and
(3) Certify the person appointed to fill the vacancy of election coordinator to the State Board of Election Commissioners within forty-five (45) days of the vacancy.
(e)(1) The county may request in writing to appoint a second qualifying individual to receive election coordinator training for the county.
(2) The Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners may permit additional persons appointed by the county board of election commissioners to attend the training program if space permits.
(3) Additional persons appointed by the county board of election commissioners to attend the training program who complete the program may be appointed to replace the election coordinator, if a need arises, without additional training.
(4) If approved by the director, any additional persons attending county election coordinator training are not eligible for mileage or compensation from the State Board of Election Commissioners.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 82-104(a)(1) as follows: "6 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(e)(4)".

Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

7 CAR § 82-105.
Required training.
(a) The person appointed as an election coordinator for each county shall attend the instructional portion of the training program conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners before the regularly scheduled preferential primary election.
(b)(1) In the event that the county board of election commissioners appoints an election coordinator after the preferential primary, the newly appointed coordinator shall attend a special training session within one hundred sixty (160) days of being appointed.
(2) The Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners shall ensure a special training is made available for any coordinator appointed more than thirty (30) days prior to the next election date established under Arkansas Code § 7-11-205.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-109

7 CAR § 82-106.
Examination and
certification of election coordinators.
(a) Having attended the instructional portion of the training program, the election coordinator shall take an examination of essential skills developed and administered by the State Board of Election Commissioners.
(b) The board shall issue a certification to each coordinator who:
(1) Attended the instructional program; and
(2) Demonstrated competency in the skills essential for conducting an election through the examination process.
(c) Certification shall be issued by the board to each designated trainee who is certified though the training program.

### 7 CAR § 82-107. Compensation

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=161&partID=549&subPartID=4573&sectionID=28779. 7 CAR § 82-107.
Compensation.
(a) Upon completion of the instructional component of the training conducted by the State Board of Election Commissioners, the one (1) person appointed to attend election coordinator training is eligible to receive a reimbursement for mileage at the rate established for state employees by state travel rules.
(b) The one (1) person appointed to attend election coordinator training who is certified under this rule and who serves as an election official through the certification of the preferential primary election results shall be eligible to receive five hundred dollars ($500) in compensation for attending training.
(c) If the county board of election commissioners appoints a new election coordinator following the preferential primary, the coordinator shall be eligible to receive mileage for required training only but is not eligible to receive additional compensation.
(d)(1) All compensation and reimbursement paid under this section shall:
(A) Be paid to the county treasurer; and
(B) Include a statement explaining:
(i) The purpose of the funds; and
(ii) Who is entitled to receive the funds under state law.
(2) Mileage for training shall be paid upon:
(A) Receipt of the reporting form approved by the State Board of Election Commissioners; and
(B) Verification that the coordinator was certified.
(3) Payment of the five hundred dollars ($500) shall be paid upon verification by the Director of the State Board of Election Commissioners that the coordinator who attended training remains an election coordinator for the county at the time the preferential primary is certified.

Codification Notes:
This section as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules contained a footnote to 7 CAR § 82-107(a) as follows: "7 A.C.A. § 7-4-109(e)(2)".

7 CAR § 90-101.
Scope.
(a) This part will set forth the procedures for processing and administering the requirement that voters verify their voter registration before they are permitted to vote.
(b) This part is in addition to, and not a substitute for, the:
(1) Laws of the State of Arkansas; and
(2) Rules promulgated by the Secretary of State.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

### 7 CAR § 90-102. Definitions

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=36014. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 90-102.
Definitions.
As used in this part:
(1)(A) “Additional identification requirement” means documentary identification, separate from that which is required to verify voter registration, that certain first-time voters who register by mail must show to cast a regular ballot during early voting or at the polls on election day.
(B) Acceptable identification documents include a copy of:
(i) Current and valid photo identification; or
(ii) A current utility bill, bank statement, government check, paycheck, or other government document that shows the name and address of the voter;
(2) “Administrator” means the administrative head of a long-term care or residential care facility licensed by the state;
(3) "Assistor" means a person named by the voter who:
(A) Is present in the polling site;
(B) Is eighteen (18) years of age or older; and
(C) Presents a document or identification that meets the requirements established in this part;
(4) “County board” means the county board of election commissioners;
(5)(A) “Documentation of residency” means a statement by the administrator of a residential care facility or long-term care facility that:
(i) Specifically identifies the full name of the resident in the body of the letter; and
(ii) Is valid for one (1) year after the date it is signed and issued.
(B) This documentation shall only be accepted if it is on the form provided by the Secretary of State;
(6) “Long-term care facility” or “Residential care facility” means:
(A) A nursing home;
(B) A residential care facility;
(C) An assisted living facility;
(D) A post-acute head injury retraining and residential care facility; or
(E) Any other facility that:
(i) Provides long-term medical or personal care; and
(ii) Is licensed by the State of Arkansas;
(7)(A) “Photo identification” means a document or identification card that satisfies all of the following conditions:
(i) Shows the name of the person to whom the document was issued;
(ii) Shows a photograph of the person to whom the document was issued;
(iii) Is issued by:
(a)
The United States;
(b)
The State of Arkansas;
(c)
An accredited postsecondary educational institution in the State of Arkansas; or
(d)
A trade school in the State of Arkansas; and
(iv) If displaying an expiration date, the document or card is not expired or expired no more than four (4) years before the date of the election in which the person seeks to vote.
(B) Documents or identification cards that comply with the above requirements for photo identification include, without limitation, the following:
(i) A driver’s license;
(ii) A photo identification card;
(iii) A concealed handgun carry license;
(iv) A United States passport;
(v) An employee badge or identification document issued to any person by an accredited postsecondary educational institution in the State of Arkansas;
(vi) A United States military identification document;
(vii) A public assistance identification card if the card shows a photograph of the person to whom the document or identification card was issued; and
(viii) A voter identification card as provided under:
(a)
Arkansas Code § 7-5-324; and
(b)
Rules propounded by the Secretary of State;
(C) A document or identification card may be presented in a digital format on an electronic device if the document or identification card:
(i) Complies with the requirements of subdivision (6)(A) of this section; and
(ii) The digital format has been approved or issued by:
(a)
The United States;
(b)
The State of Arkansas; or
(c)
An accredited postsecondary educational institution in the State of Arkansas; and
(8) “Verification of voter registration” means the process by which an individual seeking to vote presents a photo identification to an election official for the purpose of confirming that the individual physically present at the poll is the same individual to whose voter registration the vote cast will be credited.

Codification Notes:
This section, as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules, contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 90-102(1)(B) cited “[1] A.C.A. § 7-5-201(e)”.
Footnote to 7 CAR § 90-102(3)(B) cited "2 A.C.A. § 7-5-905".
Footnote to 7 CAR § 90-102(6)(B)(viii) cited "3 Ark. Const. amend. 51, § 13(b); A.C.A. § 7-1-101(38)".
History:
Ark. R. 2025-23 (eff. November 1, 2025)
Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

### 7 CAR § 90-103. Verification of voter registration required when voting at the polls

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=36015. 7 CAR § 90-103.
Verification of voter registration required when voting at the polls.
(a) All voters, except those who reside in a long-term care or residential care facility, must verify their voter registration in order to cast a regular ballot at the polls during early voting and on election day.
(b)(1) A person who resides in a long-term care or residential care facility licensed by the state may instead provide documentation from the administrator of the facility attesting that the person is a resident of the facility.
(2) This documentation shall be provided on a standardized form adopted by the Secretary of State.

Codification Notes:
This section, as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules, contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 90-103(a) cited “4 Ark. Const. amend. 51, § 13(b)(2)(B)”.

### 7 CAR § 90-104. Verification of voter registration by election officials

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=36016. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 90-104.
Verification of
voter registration by election officials.
(a)(1) The photo identification shall be used to verify the name and appearance of the voter.
(2) The precinct voter registration list shall be used to verify the address of the voter by comparing the voter’s address on the list to the address stated by the voter.
(b) The election official verifying the voter registration of a voter shall:
(1) Verify that the name on the photo identification is consistent with the name in the precinct voter registration list, allowing for abbreviations and nicknames;
(2) If the name is consistent, compare the photograph to the voter to determine whether the voter is the person depicted in the photograph, given reasonable allowances for changes in:
(A) Hair color;
(B) Glasses;
(C) Facial hair;
(D) Cosmetics;
(E) Weight;
(F) Age;
(G) Injury; and
(H) Other physical characteristics;
(3) If the election official is satisfied that the voter is the person depicted in the photograph and the name is consistent with the name in the precinct voter registration list, then issue the voter a regular ballot;
(4) If the name appearing on the voter’s photo identification varies in part from the name in the precinct voter registration list or the name as stated by the voter, an election official should issue a regular ballot only if the variation can be explained as:
(A) An abbreviation of a given name;
(B) The inclusion of a nickname on the photo identification where the name still bears substantial similarity to the voter’s name on the precinct voter registration list;
(C) A lawful change in the voter’s name due to marriage or divorce; or
(D) Other similar circumstances that satisfy the election official that the voter is the person depicted in the photograph so long as:
(i) The name still bears substantial similarity to the voter’s name on the precinct voter registration list; and
(ii) All variations are explained;
(5) If the variation is due to a change in the voter’s name and the name reflected in the precinct voter registration list is the voter’s old name, the voter should only be issued a ballot after the voter completes a voter registration application form for the purpose of updating the voter’s information;
(6) If the election official determines that the photo identification presented does not depict the voter or the name of the voter in the precinct voter registration list is insufficiently similar to the name appearing on the photo identification, the question of whether the voter is permitted to cast a regular ballot shall be referred to the election judge of that polling site who shall resolve any marginal cases of identity in favor of the voter; and
(7) If the election judge agrees that the photo identification does not depict the voter or that the name that appears on the photo identification is insufficiently similar to the name on the precinct voter registration list, the voter will have failed to verify his or her voter registration and the poll worker shall offer the voter a provisional ballot.
(c) The election judge at any polling site is the election official who is in charge of that polling site regardless of the title used locally to reference this election official.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

### 7 CAR § 90-105. Additional identification requirement for certain first-time voters

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=36017. 7 CAR § 90-105.
Additional identification requirement for certain first-time voters.
(a) Certain first-time voters who register by mail and who, when registering to vote, do not provide the required identification documentation, will be flagged on the precinct voter registration list for additional identification documentation when voting at the polls during early voting, on election day, or by voting using an absentee ballot.
(b) Before issuing a regular ballot, the poll worker shall require the flagged voter to present:
(1) A current and valid photo identification; or
(2) A copy of a current:
(A) Utility bill;
(B) Bank statement;
(C) Government check;
(D) Paycheck; or
(E) Other government document that shows the name and address of the voter.

Codification Notes:
This section, as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules, contained a footnote at 7 CAR § 90-105(c)(2) to the following: “7 A.C.A. § 7-5-201(e)”.

7 CAR § 90-106.
Failure to
verify voter registration or present other required documentation at the polls.
(a) A person voting at the polls during early voting or on election day who did not verify their voter registration or, if a resident of a long-term care or residential care facility licensed by the state, who did not provide documentation of residency from the administrator of the facility attesting that the person is a resident of the facility, shall be offered a provisional ballot.
(b) A first-time voter who registered by mail who did not present the additional identification, if required under 7 CAR § 90-104, shall be offered a provisional ballot.

### 7 CAR § 90-107. Identification required when voting absentee

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=36019. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 90-107.
Identification
required when voting absentee.
(a)(1) The absentee voter must submit verification of their voter registration when they return their absentee ballot to the county clerk’s office.
(2) A photocopy of any document that qualifies as photo identification under this part shall be sufficient to satisfy the photo identification requirement when verifying the voter’s registration.
(b) If a resident of a long-term or residential care facility licensed by the State of Arkansas does not submit photo identification in order to verify their voter registration, that resident’s ballot will still be counted as a regular ballot if the voter submits documentation of residency from the administrator of the facility attesting that the person is a resident of the facility.
(c) Active-duty members of the uniformed services and merchant marine voters and their spouses and dependents who are away from the county because of the active-duty status of a uniformed service or merchant marine member are exempt from the identification requirement for absentee ballots.
(d) The ballot of a voter who failed to verify their voter registration and who is not otherwise entitled to have his or her absentee ballot counted without verification under this section shall be considered a provisional ballot.

Codification Notes:
This section, as promulgated prior to codification into the Code of Arkansas Rules, contained footnotes to the following: Footnote to 7 CAR § 90-107(a)(1) cited “10 Ark. Const. amend. 51, § 13(b)(3); A.C.A. § 7-5-323”.

Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

### 7 CAR § 90-108. Curing provisional ballots created by the failure to verify the voter’s registration

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=36020. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Site-wide statement on every page: "Pursuant to Arkansas Code § 25-15-218, the Code of Arkansas Rules is a codification of the general and permanent rules of Arkansas agencies. This online searchable database constitutes the official version of the Code of Arkansas Rules and became effective on January 1, 2025. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 90-108.
Curing
provisional ballots created by the failure to verify the voter’s registration.
(a)
Voters at the
polls.
(1) A provisional ballot cast because a voter failed to verify their voter registration when voting at the polls during early voting or on election day shall be counted and included in the certified results of the election if:
(A) The voter’s ballot is not successfully challenged for any other reason; and
(B) The voter subsequently verifies their voter registration to either the county clerk or the county board of election commissioners by noon on the Monday following the election.
(2) The voter may verify his or her voter registration to the county board of election commissioners by presenting photo identification at any meeting of the county board of election commissioners held before noon on the Monday following the election.
(3) The voter may verify his or her voter registration to the county clerk by presenting the photo identification to the county clerk before noon on the Monday following the election.
(b)
Absentee voters.
(1) The ballot of an absentee voter that was made provisional for failure to verify that person’s voter registration when returning his or her absentee ballot shall be counted and included in the certified results of the election if:
(A) The absentee voter’s ballot is not found deficient for any other reason; and
(B) The absentee voter verifies his or her voter registration in person to either the county clerk or the county board of election commissioners by noon on the Monday following the election.
(2) The absentee voter may verify his or her voter registration to the county board of election commissioners by presenting photo identification to the county board of election commissioners at any meeting of the county board of election commissioners held before noon on the Monday following the election.
(3) The absentee voter may alternatively verify his or her voter registration to the county clerk by presenting the photo identification to the county clerk before noon on the Monday following the election.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

### 7 CAR § 90-109. Assessment of validity of photo identification submitted postelection

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=36021. 7 CAR § 90-109. Assessment of validity of photo identification submitted postelection.
(a) When verifying the voter registration of a voter who cast a provisional ballot at the poll or whose absentee ballot was deemed provisional based on the voter’s failure to verify his or her voter registration, the county clerk or county board of election commissioners shall compare the photograph to the voter to determine whether the voter is the person depicted in the photograph, given reasonable allowances for changes in:
(1) Hair color;
(2) Glasses;
(3) Facial hair;
(4) Cosmetics;
(5) Weight;
(6) Age;
(7) Injury; and
(8) Other physical characteristics.
(b) The county clerk or the county board of election commissioners verifying the voter’s registration shall:
(1) Make a photocopy of the photo identification; and
(2) Retain the copy with the voter’s provisional ballot envelope.

Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

7 CAR § 90-110.
County clerk to provide verification of voter registration to county board of election commissioners.
If a voter verifies his or her voter registration to the county clerk after the election, the county clerk who conducts the verification shall provide the copy of the voter’s photo identification to the county board of election commissioners before the county board of election commissioners certifies the election.

Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

### 7 CAR § 90-111. Assistor ID

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1116&subPartID=5540&sectionID=63378. It is updated on a weekly basis and may not reflect recent rulemaking. Please check with the respective agency for information on recent rulemaking." Page footer: "Last Updated: 9/8/2026 9:41:20 AM". Also carries a disclaimer: "The inclusion of a rule in the Code of Arkansas Rules shall not be construed to represent an expression that the rule is consistent with state or federal law or legislative intent.". Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Code of Arkansas Rules section page (the official codification under Ark. Code § 25-15-218); the publisher's History and Authority notes are appended to the rule text.*

7 CAR § 90-111. Assistor ID.
(a) An assistor named by a voter who is to assist the voter shall:
(1) Present photo identification;
(2) Present any other photo identification, other than as defined in this part; or
(3) Inform the election official that the assistor does not possess photo identification and provide the assistor’s home address, name, and date of birth under penalty of perjury.
(b)(1) An election official shall maintain a list of all assistors.
(2) If the nonelection official assistor presents photo identification, then the election official shall document the assistor’s name and type of identification and state or agency issuing the identification on the list of assistors or on the electronic poll book.
(3) If the assistor presents a photo identification, other than as defined above, (such as a Texas driver's license), the election official shall:
(A) Document the:
(i) Assistor's name;
(ii) Assistor's home address; and
(iii) Type of identification provided by the assistor, including the issuing state or agency issuing the photo identification; and
(B) Have the voter being assisted sign or mark that they are being assisted by the assistor.
(4) If the assistor does not have photo identification:
(A) The election official shall document the assistor’s:
(i) Name;
(ii) Home address; and
(iii) Date of birth;
(B) The assistor shall sign an affidavit provided and published by the State Board of Election Commissioners confirming the information provided to the election official is true and correct under penalty of perjury; and
(C) The election official shall have the voter being assisted sign or mark that the assistor is assisting the voter on the affidavit.

History:
Ark. R. 2025-23 (eff. November 1, 2025)
Authorities:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101; Arkansas Code § 7-5-310; Arkansas Constitution, Amendment 51, sec. 13

7 CAR § 91-101.
Scope.
(a) This part sets forth the procedures for:
(1) Poll watchers;
(2) Challenging votes; and
(3) Issuing, processing, and counting provisional ballots.
(b) This part is in addition to, and not a substitute for, the laws of the State of Arkansas.

Authority:
Arkansas Code § 7-4-101

### 7 CAR § 91-102. Definitions

*Source: https://codeofarrules.arkansas.gov/Rules/Rule?levelType=section&titleID=7&chapterID=112&subChapterID=164&partID=1117&subPartID=5541&sectionID=36024. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10).
